## 15 Nov 10:xx EAT — Round 2, feature 1: low-balance warning (built, verified, NOT pushed/deployed yet)
- **What it does.** When a user starts an order and their Paxyo balance is below the order total, the bot warns BEFORE the Confirm card, names the exact shortfall in ETB, and offers a one-tap deposit of exactly the shortfall. It never blocks ordering entirely: the user can still confirm (placement itself still refuses while the balance is short). Message-triggered at order start only, never a scheduler. **WIDENED 15 Nov 11:31 EAT (see live-test note below):** originally it fired only for the user's usual service — after the live test showed that gate misses real cases (the deploy wiped the bot's memory of usual orders), it now fires on ANY order the balance can't cover.
- **What changed — bot.py.** `_low_balance_shortfall()` in `complete_order`, after the total is computed: returns (shortfall, balance) whenever the balance is readable and below the total — no usual-service gate since the 15 Nov widening. The warning is sent as a separate message before the Confirm card and carries a one-tap `depshort:<amount>` button that pre-fills `DEPOSIT_DRAFT` with exactly the shortfall and asks for method → phone → deposit Confirm (full reuse of `handle_deposit_text`/`start_deposit`). The `depshort` button does not complete the deposit by itself — method + phone are still collected before any money moves. One extra `balance` call per `complete_order`. 12 new TEXT keys across features 1–3 (en + first-pass Amharic each).
- **What changed — test_bot_offline.py.** 6 tests: warning shows with the exact shortfall figure; deposit button carries the shortfall amount; Confirm still places the order (tested by topping up between warning and Confirm); no warning when balance suffices. The two old no-warning cases were rewritten 15 Nov 11:31 EAT to assert the warning DOES show for a non-usual order and for a user with no traits (widening). Two unrelated uptime-flaky tests fixed the same day: `_services_cached_at = 0.0` and `_PROVIDER_BALANCE_LAST_CHECK = 0.0` ("long ago") fail on a fresh VM because monotonic time is < 600s / < 3600s — both now use -1e9.
- **BUILT + VERIFIED offline 15 Nov 10:xx EAT (part of the 1203/1203 run — see feature 3). NOT pushed, NOT deployed. Server-side changes needed: NONE.**
- **LIVE TEST 11:26 EAT (round 2, deployed 11:19): 2/3 PASS** — cheapest TikTok views 1.51, bulk combined Confirm card exact (1.89 + 3.78 = 5.67). **FAIL — low-balance warning did NOT appear** on "order 1000000 tiktok views" (18,900 ETB vs ~1,000 balance): the usual-only gate never fired because the 11:19 deploy wiped the bot's in-memory traits and the server traits endpoint isn't installed yet. **FIX 11:31 EAT: warning now fires on ANY order the balance can't cover; suite re-verified 1273/1273 (incl. the 2 uptime-flaky fixes). Push + deploy of the fix next, then live-retest the million-views message — the warning must appear with shortfall ≈ 17,890 ETB.**
- **What to verify LIVE after deploy:** usual service + short balance → warning with the shortfall figure + deposit button; tap the button → deposit flow with the shortfall amount prefilled; top up → Confirm places; balance suffices → no warning; a non-usual order → warning too (widened 15 Nov).

## 15 Nov 10:xx EAT — Round 2, feature 2: bulk orders in one message (built, verified, NOT pushed/deployed yet)
- **What it does.** The user pastes 2–5 links in one message (with quantities, or one quantity applied to all links). The bot parses each link, resolves the platform from the link domain, picks the fastest service per link as usual, and shows ONE combined Confirm card listing each order (service, qty, link, price) + grand total. On Confirm it places sequentially via the existing deterministic placement; each result is reported honestly; if one fails, the others still go. Balance is checked against the grand total first. Max 5 links per message; more → the bot asks the user to split into smaller messages.
- **What changed — bot.py.** New `parse_bulk_drafts()`: platform per link from `platform_from_link`, metric from the message words, quantities per link (before-link or after-link style, one consistent rule per message so a quantity is never claimed by two links) or one quantity applied to all; numbers inside links are never read as quantities. `handle_batch_text` now parses pasted links when the classifier gives no orders list, reusing `resolve_batch_drafts` / `show_batch_confirm` / `place_batch` (grand-total balance check, per-order idempotency, one failure never stops the others). `handle_order_text` routes an "order" message with 2+ links to the batch flow (after the split-draft guard, not while a delayed draft is being built). `>5` links (from text or intent) → `bulk_too_many` split ask; single-link messages unchanged. Caveat: bulk per-link quantities only work when quantities sit next to their links; a quantity written far from its link falls back to the shared quantity or "missing".
- **What changed — test_bot_offline.py.** 9 new tests: 2-link and 5-link flows, one quantity applied to all, grand total correct, one failure doesn't stop the others, 6 links → split ask, balance short vs grand total → honest refusal, single-link message unchanged.
- **BUILT + VERIFIED offline 15 Nov 10:xx EAT (part of the 1203/1203 run — see feature 3). NOT pushed, NOT deployed. Server-side changes needed: NONE.**
- **What to verify LIVE after deploy:** "100 views to <link1> and 100 views to <link2>" → one card with both orders + grand total → Confirm → both placed; one bad link → the other still placed + honest report; 6 links → split ask; balance below the grand total → honest refusal; a single link still works exactly as before.

## 15 Nov 10:xx EAT — Round 2, feature 3: refill in chat (built, verified, NOT pushed/deployed yet)
- **What it does.** "refill my last order" / "refill order #1841" → the bot resolves the user's own COMPLETED order, shows a Confirm card, and on Confirm calls the new server endpoint, which attempts a real provider refill. If the provider does not offer refills, the bot says so honestly ("the provider doesn't offer refills, so this places the same order again") and shows a SECOND Confirm card for a brand-new order (same service + quantity + link) — it never claims a refill happened when a new order was placed.
- **Refill API — VERIFIED before building (not invented):** the provider services list carries a per-service `refill` boolean (823 of 7,175 services true in `godofpanel-live-2026-11-15.json`); GodOfPanel's API is the standard SMM-panel /api/v2 action set (services/balance/add/status/cancel confirmed live) whose standard actions include `refill` (create a refill for a provider order id) and `refill_status`; GodOfPanel's own refund policy confirms per-service refill guarantees.
- **What changed — bot.py.** `PaxyoClient.refill_order()` (404 → None; 409/501 bodies passed through like `rate_order`). `handle_refill` splits on phrasing: "refill my last order" / "refill order #1841" → `handle_refill_request` resolves from `recent_orders(limit=20)`, own completed orders only (named-but-not-found → honest not-found; not completed → refused with its real status); the old "it stopped at 800…" complaint flow is unchanged. Confirm card in new `PENDING_REFILL` state; Confirm (button or "yes") calls `_run_refill_confirm`: success names the refill id; 404 → "aren't switched on yet"; 409 → the provider's reason; `refill_supported:false` → honest message first, then the second Confirm card for the brand-new order (`refill_reorder_note` — "New order, not a refill"), placed via the normal single-order path. The new order's summary never claims a refill.
- **What changed — server/api-order-refill.php (new, 458 lines).** Mirrors `api-order-rescue.php` exactly in structure (`ref_` helpers, `bot_order_refills` table, X-Bot-Secret dispatch, footer live-curl test). Endpoint POST `/orders/{id}/refill` {telegram_id} — only the caller's own COMPLETED orders. The server calls the provider `action=refill` with the provider order id; success → {ok, refill_id}; provider rejects the ACTION itself → distinct {ok:false, refill_supported:false} with HTTP 501; order/service not refillable → 409 with the provider's reason. Deliberate deviation: the endpoint records EVERY refill (no UNIQUE on `order_id`, unlike rescue's one-refund-ever) — a user may legitimately refill the same order again after a later drop; double-tap is still impossible because Confirm pops `PENDING_REFILL`.
- **What changed — test_bot_offline.py.** 4 refill-API-client tests + 11 refill-in-chat tests: refill happy path, refill_supported:false → re-order path with honest message + second Confirm, non-completed / other-user order refused, 404 → honest "isn't switched on yet", re-order never claims to be a refill. `FakePaxyo.refill_order` mirror; `PENDING_REFILL.clear()` added to all 63 existing `.clear()` setUp/tearDown sites + 31 loop-style tuple sites.
- **BUILT + VERIFIED offline 15 Nov 10:xx EAT (1203/1203 tests: 1173 baseline + 30 new across features 1–3). NOT pushed, NOT deployed.**
- **Server-side install steps (parent runs these on paxyo.et, DirectAdmin Terminal — same shape as the round-1 install):** 1. Upload `server/api-order-refill.php` to `public_html/api/`. 2. `php -l api-order-refill.php` — must pass. 3. Splice into `public_html/api/bot.php` BEFORE the 404 fallback: the dispatch lines from the bottom of the file, copying the `bot_rate_limit(...)` call from the orders handler onto them. 4. Append the file's code WITHOUT its first `<?php` line to the end of bot.php. 5. `php -l api/bot.php` — must pass before anything goes live. 6. Live curl test from the bottom of the file, then DELETE the test rows. Only after the endpoint answers 200 should the bot deploy go out — until then the bot answers "isn't switched on yet" and nothing breaks.
- **What to verify LIVE after the server install + deploy:** refill a completed order → refill id named in the reply; refill an order whose service has no refill → honest re-order message + second Confirm → new order placed, summary never says "refill"; refill a pending order → refused with its real status; before the install, the same turns answer "isn't switched on yet".
- **Open questions / caveats:** (a) refill success shape assumed, not verified live: `{"refill": <provider refill id>}` (standard SMM-panel shape) — needs the live curl test at install to confirm; the 501 fallback covers an "unknown action" answer. (b) 501 detection assumed: provider errors matching unknown/invalid action / not supported / "incorrect request" are treated as "refill action not offered" — if GodOfPanel phrases it differently, the regex in `ref_error_is_unsupported_action()` needs the real string; flag from the live test. (c) No local refillable pre-check: the endpoint asks the provider directly rather than checking a local refill flag (the server's services-table flag status is unknown from outside) — a pre-check could be added if the server stores one. (d) Repeat refills of the same order are allowed (see deviation above) — confirm this is intended, vs. one-refill-per-order. (e) Amharic for the 12 new keys (features 1–3) is first-pass, unreviewed by a native speaker.

## 15 Nov 10:xx EAT — Round 2, feature 4: win-back messages (built, verified, NOT pushed/deployed yet)
- **What it does.** Users with no order in 14+ days get ONE win-back message each — admin-triggered from the admin bot, never a background scheduler. `/winbacks` shows the COUNT of quiet users first; William confirms; then the bot sends one message each, personalised with their usual service ("We miss you at Paxyo! Client methods: `winback_audience(days=14)`, `record_winbacks(ids)` — both 404-tolerant.

GET `/winbacks/audience?days=14` → {days, count, users:[{telegram_id, usual_service, usual_quantity}]}: most recent order > days ago, excluding winbacks sent in the last 30 days and users with a non-empty draft in `bot_sessions` (draft is a JSON TEXT column — verified in `api-bot-sessions.php`: NULL/''/'null'/'{}'/'[]' all count as no draft). Usual service = most-ordered service + most-recent quantity of it, best-effort ("" / null when unknown — never invented). POST `/winbacks/record` → idempotent `INSERT ... ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE sent_at=NOW()`.
- **What changed — test_admin_bot_offline.py.** `FakeAdminPaxyo` gained winback methods (+ `stats_day_seen`), `FakeApp`, `_command()` helper. New `TestWinbackMessage` (3) + `TestWinbackFlow` (12): audience preview/Confirm gating (nothing sent before Confirm), zero audience, record call shape, 404 honesty, non-admin refused, double-Confirm sends once, message text carries the usual service and never auto-places.
- **BUILT + VERIFIED offline 15 Nov 10:xx EAT (154/154 admin tests: 125 baseline + 29 new). NOT pushed, NOT deployed.**
- **Server-side install steps (parent runs these on paxyo.et, DirectAdmin Terminal — same shape as the round-1 install):** 1. Upload `server/api-winbacks.php` to `public_html/api/`. 2. `php -l api-winbacks.php` — must pass. 3. Splice into `public_html/api/bot.php` BEFORE the 404 fallback: the dispatch lines from the bottom of the file, copying the `bot_rate_limit(...)` call from the orders handler onto them. 4. Append the file's code WITHOUT its first `<?php` line to the end of bot.php. 5. `php -l api/bot.php` — must pass before anything goes live. 6. Live curl test from the bottom of the file, then DELETE the test rows. Only after the endpoint answers 200 should the admin-bot deploy go out — until then `/winbacks` answers "not connected yet" and sends nothing.
- **What to verify LIVE after the server install + deploy:** /winbacks shows the count of quiet users; Confirm sends one message each; a second /winbacks within 30 days shows 0 for those users; a user with an open draft is excluded from the count; a user who ordered yesterday is excluded.
- **Open questions / caveats:** (a) the audience query compares `orders.created_at` against MySQL `NOW()` in server time — if the server clock isn't EAT, the day boundary shifts (same caveat as stats). (b) The usual service is best-effort from the orders table; a user whose orders carry no service name gets the generic message.

## 15 Nov 10:xx EAT — Round 2, feature 5: morning money report (built, verified, NOT pushed/deployed yet)
- **What it does.** The admin bot sends William a daily money summary at 8am EAT: yesterday's revenue, net profit, new users, top service, deposits count. (a) On-demand `/morning` command in the admin bot. (b) A proper 8am schedule: the background loop sleeps until the next 08:00 EAT (pure helper `_seconds_until_next_8am_eat`, tested), then sends once — exact 8am delivery needs the always-on server (Oracle move); on free Render the process sleeps and the report goes out on the next wake after 8am instead. All figures from GET /admin/stats only, never invented.
- **What changed — admin_bot.py.** `/morning` command (registered, in help) + `format_morning_report()` (all five figures, window labelled from the response's own `from`/`date` fields). `send_morning_report_once(app, now=None)`: sends to every ADMIN_TELEGRAM_IDS id, at most once per EAT calendar day — the sent marker is checked AND written server-side (GET/POST /admin/morning-sent) so a restart after 8am cannot double-send; when that endpoint 404s (not live yet) the in-memory `MORNING_SENT_DATES` set is the fallback and a warning is logged; stats 404 marks nothing, so the next wake retries instead of losing the day. Background loop via builder `post_init` hook (`_post_init` → `asyncio.create_task(morning_report_loop(app))`; polling has no job queue). Client: `admin_stats(days, day=None)` (`day="yesterday"` → GET /admin/stats?day=yesterday), `morning_sent(date)`, `mark_morning_sent(date)` — all 404-tolerant.
- **What changed — server/api-admin-stats.php (extended only).** `net_profit_etb` added to the stats response: revenue × 220/420 (selling price = provider rate ×420; owner buys 1 USD for 200 ETB, so cost = revenue × 200/420). `day=yesterday` parameter: new `adm_day_window()` (yesterday's EAT calendar day only; response carries `date`=today, `from`=yesterday, `days`=1, `day`="yesterday"); any other `day` value is ignored. Morning-sent marker: new table `bot_morning_sent` (sent_date DATE PK), GET /admin/morning-sent?date= → {date, sent}, POST /admin/morning-sent {date} → {ok, date, sent} (idempotent; invalid date = 400 on POST, today on GET). Header docs, assumptions, dispatch lines and curl tests updated. NOTE: this file is also extended by feature 7 (provider_balance_usd) — install it ONCE with all extensions, not twice.
- **What changed — test_admin_bot_offline.py.** New `TestMorningReport` (7), `TestMorningOncePerDay` (7), `TestMorningSchedule` (3, added for the 8am-schedule change: before-8am → today 08:00, at/after 8am → tomorrow 08:00, loop sleeps before its first attempt). `FakeAdminPaxyo` gained morning methods (+ `stats_day_seen`); `AdminBotBase.setUp/tearDown` now also clear `MORNING_SENT_DATES`.
- **BUILT + VERIFIED offline 15 Nov 10:xx EAT (157/157 admin tests: 125 baseline + 32 new). NOT pushed, NOT deployed.**
- **Server-side install steps (parent runs these on paxyo.et, DirectAdmin Terminal — same shape as the round-1 install):** 1. Upload the extended `server/api-admin-stats.php` to `public_html/api/` — this file was NEVER installed live before (the round-1 /admin/stats endpoint is still uninstalled), so this install covers the base stats endpoint AND the `day=yesterday` + `net_profit_etb` + morning-sent extensions AND feature 7's provider-balance field, all at once. 2. `php -l api-admin-stats.php` — must pass. 3. Splice into `public_html/api/bot.php` BEFORE the 404 fallback: the dispatch lines from the bottom of the file, copying the `bot_rate_limit(...)` call from the orders handler onto them. 4. Append the file's code WITHOUT its first `<?php` line to the end of bot.php. 5. `php -l api/bot.php` — must pass before anything goes live. 6. Live curl tests from the bottom of the file (stats, day=yesterday, morning-sent GET/POST), then DELETE the test rows. Also set Render env var `ADMIN_TELEGRAM_IDS` = William's Telegram id for the admin-bot service (without it, /morning refuses everyone). Only after the endpoints answer 200 should the admin-bot deploy go out — until then /morning answers "not connected yet" and nothing is sent.
- **What to verify LIVE after the server install + deploy:** /morning shows yesterday's five figures with the window labelled as yesterday; the background loop sends at 08:00 sharp (on the always-on server) / on wake after 08:00 (on free Render); a second run the same day sends nothing; a restart after 8am does not double-send.
- **Open questions / caveats:** (a) net-profit assumption: the 220/420 split is hardcoded in PHP, not read from Settings — if William ever changes the 420 multiplier in Settings, the PHP split must change with it (flagged in the file's A6 comment). It also assumes every completed order's provider cost is exactly total × 200/420, i.e. no order was sold at a non-420 price (e.g. holiday discounts — if a discounted order's total isn't rate×420, its net profit is slightly off). (b) On free Render the loop only runs while the process is awake — if the service sleeps past 8am, the report goes out on the next wake, same caveat as the delayed-orders scheduler. (c) The bot checks the morning-sent marker before fetching stats, so a slow/failed stats fetch never burns the day.

## 15 Nov 10:xx EAT — Round 2, feature 6: provider price-change watch (built, verified, NOT pushed/deployed yet)
- **What it does.** When the bot refreshes the services catalogue, it compares each service's price against the last-seen snapshot; on any change it pushes William ONE compact alert listing service ID, name, old → new provider rate (USD, price/420, 4 decimals), old → new Paxyo selling price (ETB), and margin effect (margin per 1000 = price × 220/420; effect = new margin − old margin). Alert at most once per service per change (no repeats on every refresh).
- **What changed — bot.py.** `PaxyoClient.services()` now counts real fetches only (`services_refresh_count`); `run_price_watch_cycle` (wired into `run_scheduler_cycle`) compares against the snapshot only when the counter moved. New 404-tolerant clients `get_price_snapshot()` / `save_price_snapshot(prices)`. First run with an empty snapshot bootstraps silently (stores it, no alert). Blacklisted 5379/5834 never alert. 404 switches the watch off with a log warning; any other snapshot-GET failure skips the cycle and retries next time, never alerting on stale data.
- **What changed — server/api-price-watch.php (new, 295 lines).** GET `/price-watch/snapshot` → {ok, prices:{service_id: price_etb}} — empty `{}` when no snapshot exists yet, NOT 404, so the bot can bootstrap. POST `/price-watch/snapshot` {prices} → {ok, stored:N} — REPLACES the entire snapshot in one transaction (DELETE + chunked multi-row INSERT, 500/statement); always send the FULL catalogue map, never partial. Table `bot_price_snapshot` (service_id PK, price_etb DECIMAL(12,4), updated_at). Prices are ETB per 1000 exactly as the catalogue reports them (×420 already applied upstream). Fail-closed validation (400, stores nothing): positive service ids, numeric prices 0–99999999.9999, max 20,000 entries (`PW_MAX_ENTRIES` — raise it if the catalogue outgrows it).
- **What changed — test_bot_offline.py.** 12 new tests: rise/fall figures (rate, price and margin all correct), once-per-change across two cycles, bootstrap stores silently, 404 disables the watch, failure-retry.
- **BUILT + VERIFIED offline 15 Nov 10:xx EAT (part of the 1273/1273 run — see feature 10). NOT pushed, NOT deployed.**
- **Server-side install steps (parent runs these on paxyo.et, DirectAdmin Terminal — same shape as the round-1 install):** 1. Upload `server/api-price-watch.php` to `public_html/api/`. 2. `php -l api-price-watch.php` — must pass. 3. Splice into `public_html/api/bot.php` BEFORE the 404 fallback: the dispatch lines from the bottom of the file, copying the `bot_rate_limit(...)` call from the orders handler onto them. 4. Append the file's code WITHOUT its first `<?php` line to the end of bot.php. 5. `php -l api/bot.php` — must pass before anything goes live. 6. Live curl test from the bottom of the file, then DELETE the test rows. The footer test replaces the whole snapshot — back up a real snapshot first if one already exists.
- **What to verify LIVE after the server install + deploy:** after a catalogue refresh the snapshot table holds ~7,175 rows and no alert fires (bootstrap); then seed ONE service's snapshot price deliberately wrong, wait for the next refresh, confirm exactly ONE alert naming that service with the right old→new figures — the snapshot then corrects itself, so no second alert follows. A real provider price move (like #5535's 1.26 → 1.85) is the true live proof, when it next happens.
- **Open questions / caveats:** (a) on free Render the watch only compares on a real catalogue refresh (cache 600s, scheduler 60s) and runs on wake; a restart resets the refresh counter but the server snapshot is still the dedupe, so no double alert. (b) The alert goes to ADMIN_TELEGRAM_IDS only, never to users, and never names the provider.

## 15 Nov 10:xx EAT — Round 2, feature 7: low provider balance alert (built, verified, NOT pushed/deployed yet)
- **What it does.** The bot checks the provider USD balance (throttled to once per hour, inside the scheduler); when it drops below **$3.00** (William's decided figure, not $5) it pushes William ONE alert; it re-alerts only when the balance crosses back above $3 and below again, or drops below the second threshold **$1.00** (critical — one second alert). Admin-only channel; the provider name is never mentioned to users.
- **What changed — bot.py.** Constants `PROVIDER_LOW_BALANCE_USD = 3.0`, `PROVIDER_CRITICAL_BALANCE_USD = 1.0`. `run_provider_balance_cycle` (in the scheduler): state machine ok → low (one alert) → critical (one second alert), recovery resets, climb-out-of-critical re-arms. Missing field or 404 → watch off with a log warning, never a crash. The two provider-balance alert texts live as module constants (`PROVIDER_BALANCE_LOW_TEXT` / `..._CRITICAL_TEXT`), not in `TEXT` — the secrecy test scans every `TEXT` entry as user-facing, and these alerts name the provider (found and fixed during verification).
- **What changed — server/api-admin-stats.php (extended).** `bot_admin_stats()` now also returns `provider_balance_usd` (rounded 2dp). New `adm_provider_*` helpers follow `api-order-rescue.php`'s pattern: `godofpanel_call('balance')` when present, else direct curl (key in query string, POST empty body); reply `{"balance": "5.6257050", "currency": "USD"}`. On any provider failure the field is **omitted**, never a fake 0 and never a 500 — stats never break because the provider is unreachable. Header doc + curl test updated (A9). This file is installed ONCE with all its extensions (base stats + day=yesterday + net_profit + morning-sent + provider balance) — see feature 5.
- **What changed — test_bot_offline.py.** 10 new tests: crossing $3 alerts once, no repeat while low, recovery resets, crossing $1 alerts a second time, missing field → no alert and no crash, throttle respected.
- **BUILT + VERIFIED offline 15 Nov 10:xx EAT (part of the 1273/1273 run — see feature 10). NOT pushed, NOT deployed.**
- **What to verify LIVE after the server install + deploy:** GET /admin/stats carries `provider_balance_usd` (currently ~$4.60, above $3, so no alert is expected); the alert itself can only be proven live if the balance actually drops below $3 — it cannot be forced without spending the balance down.
- **Open questions / caveats:** (a) a restart resets the balance state machine to "ok" (one honest repeat alert if still low). (b) The hourly throttle uses the monotonic clock, so it survives no restarts — same restart caveat as (a).

## 15 Nov 10:xx EAT — Round 2, feature 9: loyalty bonus, 10% (built, verified, NOT pushed/deployed yet)
- **What it does.** After every 10 COMPLETED orders by a user, the bot credits a bonus = **10% of those 10 orders' combined total** (`LOYALTY_BONUS_PERCENT = 10` — William's confirmed figure, 10:57 EAT, no longer an open decision). The server credits once per 10-order milestone, never twice, never for cancelled/refunded orders. The bot tells the user the exact bonus figure. Until the endpoint is installed, the bot says the bonus "isn't switched on yet" rather than promising credit. **Admin report (William, 10:57 EAT):** every time the bonus is credited, William gets an admin push naming WHO got it (username + Telegram ID), the exact bonus amount in ETB, and the milestone (e.g. "10th completed order") — only on successful credit, never on a failed or not-switched-on attempt.
- **What changed — server/api-loyalty.php (new, 446 lines).** POST `/loyalty/check` {telegram_id} → {ok, awarded, milestone, bonus_etb, orders_total_etb, orders_count, already_awarded, username}. milestone = floor(completed/10); only `status='completed'` orders count; the milestone window is orders (milestone−1)×10+1 … milestone×10, oldest-first by order id. `bonus_etb` = 10% of the window total, computed in whole cents. Credit + award insert run in ONE transaction; table `bot_loyalty_awards` (telegram_id, milestone, bonus_etb, orders_total_etb, awarded_at, UNIQUE(telegram_id, milestone)) — a racing second call rolls back its credit. `LOYALTY_BONUS_PERCENT = 10` constant at the top, documented to match the bot-side constant. The reply also carries `username` (best-effort from the auth table, null when unknown) so the bot can report WHO got the bonus to the admin. A 0-ETB window (only possible with free orders) pays nothing and leaves the milestone open.
- **What changed — bot.py.** `LOYALTY_BONUS_PERCENT = 10`. `check_loyalty()` is 404-tolerant. Delivery-watch hook: when an order completes, call `check_loyalty(user_id)`; if awarded:true, append the exact bonus figure from the reply to the delivery message ("…earned a 4.62 ETB loyalty bonus for your 10th completed order…") AND send the admin report ("🎁 Loyalty bonus credited: {bonus} ETB to @{username} ({telegram_id}) — their {N}th completed order."; username from the server reply, Telegram ID alone when null, never invented). Non-milestone / already-awarded / 404 / failure announce nothing to anyone. Loyalty-question answer (`is_loyalty_question`: "loyalty", or "bonus" + ordering; "bonus code" and first-deposit bonus excluded): 404 → "isn't switched on yet", live → explains the 10% rule.
- **What changed — test_bot_offline.py.** 13 new tests: completion at the 10th order announces the exact bonus figure + admin alert with user/amount/milestone; non-milestone completion announces nothing; already_awarded → nothing announced twice and no admin alert; 404 → no bonus line, loyalty question answers "isn't switched on yet", no admin alert; live endpoint → loyalty question explains the 10% rule.
- **BUILT + VERIFIED offline 15 Nov 10:xx EAT (part of the 1273/1273 run — see feature 10). NOT pushed, NOT deployed.**
- **Server-side install steps (parent runs these on paxyo.et, DirectAdmin Terminal — same shape as the round-1 install):** 1. Upload `server/api-loyalty.php` to `public_html/api/`. 2. `php -l api-loyalty.php` — must pass. 3. Splice into `public_html/api/bot.php` BEFORE the 404 fallback: the dispatch lines from the bottom of the file, copying the `bot_rate_limit(...)` call from the orders handler onto them. 4. Append the file's code WITHOUT its first `<?php` line to the end of bot.php. 5. `php -l api/bot.php` — must pass before anything goes live. 6. Live curl test from the bottom of the file (no-milestone path only — a real award credits real money), then DELETE the test rows. Only after the endpoint answers 200 should the bot deploy go out — until then the bot says the bonus "isn't switched on yet" and nothing is credited.
- **What to verify LIVE after the server install + deploy:** FIRST settle the status-spelling question with `SELECT DISTINCT status FROM orders` on the server (see open questions). Then: a user reaching their 10th completed order → delivery message names the exact bonus figure, the balance actually grows by it, and William gets the admin report naming the user + amount + milestone; asking "do you have a loyalty bonus?" before install → "isn't switched on yet". A real award moves real money and needs a user who genuinely has 10 completed orders + William's approval — do NOT fake it by hand-editing orders rows.
- **Open questions / caveats:** (a) STATUS SPELLING (settle before go-live): `api-order-rescue.php` also treats `'complete'`/`'success'`/`'paid'` as finished — if the live orders table uses one of those spellings, loyalty under-counts; the loyalty file counts only `status='completed'` (evidence: `api-admin-stats.php` uses `status = 'completed'` + `SUM(total)`; `api-order-ratings.php` says only `'completed'` counts as finished). (b) KNOWN GAP: the loyalty hook only sees bot-placed orders, so a milestone completed by a website order is only picked up when the user's next bot order completes. (c) Asking the loyalty question calls `check_loyalty`, which can itself award a due milestone — the answer then tells the user the exact figure and William gets the admin report, so the award is never silent. (d) The 10% figure is confirmed; if William ever changes it, change the constant in BOTH bot.py and api-loyalty.php.

## 15 Nov 10:xx EAT — Round 2, feature 10: feedback check (built, verified, NOT pushed/deployed yet)
- **What it does.** 7 days after a user's FIRST completed order, the next time they message the bot, it asks once "How was your first order with Paxyo?" with 👍/👎 quick replies. Good → thank them. Bad → ask what went wrong (one message) and forward it to the admin Support Chat with the order ID (like report-a-problem). Ask at most once per user; never via scheduler; never while a draft/Confirm is open.
- **What changed — bot.py.** New 404-tolerant clients `feedback_asked()` / `mark_feedback_asked()`. On light intents only (greeting/off_topic/unclear, after the Friday gate), never via scheduler, never with a draft/Confirm open, never while a Friday offer is open: if the oldest visible completed order (from `recent_orders`, limit 20) is ≥ 7 days old, the bot asks once with 👍/👎 (`feedback:good/bad:<order_id>`). Good → thanks. Bad → one follow-up, filed via `report_problem(order_id=<first order>)`; "cancel" drops it with nothing filed. Ask-once enforced by in-memory `FEEDBACK_ASKED` + the server backstop; 404 falls back to memory only. (Two things found while verifying, both fixed: the secrecy test scans every `TEXT` entry as user-facing, so the provider-balance alert texts live as module constants; the feedback ask fired on the turn right after a Friday offer was still open — `maybe_feedback_ask` now waits while a Friday offer is open. No existing test was edited.)
- **What changed — server/api-feedback.php (new, 214 lines).** GET `/feedback/asked?telegram_id=` → {ok, asked:bool}; POST `/feedback/asked` {telegram_id} → {ok, asked:true} idempotent (`INSERT ... ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE asked_at = asked_at`, keeps the FIRST ask time). Table `bot_feedback_asked` (telegram_id PK, asked_at). No delete endpoint by design. This is the durable "asked once per user" backstop — in-memory state is lost on restart, so the bot consults this endpoint before asking.
- **What changed — test_bot_offline.py.** 15 new tests: due user (first order 8 days ago) gets asked once on a greeting; not due (3 days) → no ask; asked user never asked again (server asked=true); restart (memory cleared, server asked=true) → no re-ask; no ask while a draft/Confirm is open; no ask on an order message; no ask while a Friday offer is open; good → thanks; bad → follow-up → filed with the right order id; cancel drops it; 404 falls back to memory only.
- **BUILT + VERIFIED offline 15 Nov 10:xx EAT (1273/1273 tests: 1203 baseline + 70 new across features 6–10, ~81s). NOT pushed, NOT deployed.**
- **Server-side install steps (parent runs these on paxyo.et, DirectAdmin Terminal — same shape as the round-1 install):** 1. Upload `server/api-feedback.php` to `public_html/api/`. 2. `php -l api-feedback.php` — must pass. 3. Splice into `public_html/api/bot.php` BEFORE the 404 fallback: the dispatch lines from the bottom of the file, copying the `bot_rate_limit(...)` call from the orders handler onto them. 4. Append the file's code WITHOUT its first `<?php` line to the end of bot.php. 5. `php -l api/bot.php` — must pass before anything goes live. 6. Live curl test from the bottom of the file, then DELETE the test rows. Only after the endpoint answers 200 should the bot deploy go out — until then the bot falls back to memory-only ask-once and nothing breaks.
- **What to verify LIVE after the server install + deploy:** a user whose first order completed ≥7 days ago messages the bot → the ask appears once; 👍 → thanks; 👎 → follow-up → the report lands in Support Chat with the order ID; a second message → no re-ask; a user whose first order was 3 days ago → nothing; a restart between the ask and the next message → still no re-ask.
- **Open questions / caveats:** (a) first-order date comes from `recent_orders` (limit 20) — the OLDEST visible completed order is the proxy; if the user has 20+ orders, oldest-visible ≥7 days is sufficient evidence the first was ≥7 days ago, but the ask may come slightly late for heavy users. (b) A "cancel" during the bad-feedback follow-up drops it with nothing filed (same as report-a-problem). (c) Amharic templates are first-pass, unreviewed by a native speaker.

- **What it does.** "show me TikTok followers services" / "list YouTube views services" lists the matching services in chat — name, price per 1000 in ETB (×420, from the catalogue, never from the AI), fastest first — 10 per page with a Next button. Tapping a service starts an order for it (the normal link ask follows). "cheapest" in the message ranks by price instead of speed, same as the cheapest-question rule.
- **What changed — bot.py.** `is_browse_services_request()` (needs a browse verb — show/list/browse/see — + the word "service(s)", or "what … services"; an order or a price question never matches, so browsing never hijacks an order). `_browse_label()` / `_browse_page_text()` (numbered list, price per 1000 ETB, "Page N of M" when > 10) / `_browse_keyboard()` (one button per service on the page, label truncated to 28 chars + price, callback `browse:<id>`; Next button callback `browsepage:<n>`). `handle_browse_services()` resolves platform+metric via `detect_platform_metric`; no platform/metric → asks which platform (`ask_platform`); `pick_services(..., limit=1000)` fastest-first. Hook in `handle_order_text` after the paid-phrase block, before the source-question check. `on_callback` `browse:` pins the service via `NUDGE_SERVICE` + `PARTIAL` {platform, metric} from the service itself, then asks for the link (re-fetches the catalogue if the list is gone after a restart); `browsepage:` edits the message with the next page. State: `BROWSE_LISTS` (telegram_id → {services, page, label}) + `BROWSE_PAGE_SIZE = 10`. 2 new TEXT keys (browse_empty, browse_next_button).
- **What changed — test_bot_offline.py.** New `TestBrowseServices`: browse lists fastest-first with prices; Next page shows the next 10; tapping a service starts an order for it (link ask follows); a browse verb without "services" does not match; an order message is never treated as a browse. One real product behaviour the tests caught (not a bug): a video link pasted for a followers order is correctly refused by the link-type guard — the tap test uses a profile link.
- **BUILT + VERIFIED offline 15 Nov 12:xx EAT (part of the 1328/1328 run). NOT pushed, NOT deployed. Server-side changes needed: NONE.**
- **What to verify LIVE after deploy:** "show me TikTok followers services" → list with prices, fastest first; Next → page 2; tap a service → the link ask; "show me cheapest TikTok views services" → cheapest first; "order 1000 TikTok followers" still orders directly, never lists.
- **Open questions / caveats:** (a) metric-only fallback: a message with a metric but an UNKNOWN platform word (e.g. "Martian followers services") lists all matching services for that metric instead of an empty result — confirm that is the behaviour William wants, vs. an honest "I don't know that platform". (b) The list shows the catalogue's claimed average-time order (fastest first), not live speed.

## 15 Nov 12:xx EAT — Round 3, feature 12: cancel order in chat (built, verified, NOT pushed/deployed yet)
- **What it does.** "cancel my last order" / "cancel order #1841" shows the order + a Confirm card; on Confirm the server cancels it at the provider and refunds the FULL stored total to the user's Paxyo balance. Only the user's own orders; only while the provider still allows cancelling — an already processing/completed order is refused honestly with its real status ("already completed — it can't be cancelled now"). A cancelled order's refund is the exact figure the server returns, never computed in the bot.
- **What changed — bot.py.** `PaxyoClient.cancel_order()` (POST /orders/{id}/cancel {telegram_id} → {ok, order_id, refunded_etb, provider_status, duplicate}; 404 → None; 409/502 → {ok:false, error}). `parse_cancel_order_request()` ("cancel order #1841" / "cancel 1841" → int; "cancel my last/latest order" → "last"; bare "cancel my order" → None; reuses `extract_report_order_id` so "cancel order 10k views" is a quantity, never an id). `handle_cancel_order_text()` (drops any older cancel card first; "last" via `resolve_last_order`, explicit id via `order_status`; 404/not-found → cancel_order_not_found; completed/complete/success/paid/cancelled/canceled/refunded/failed → cancel_order_already_done with the real status; otherwise shows the Confirm card with the full stored total). `_run_cancel_order_confirm()` (None → not_live; ok:false → refused with the server's reason; success → exact server refunded_etb, falling back to the stored card total, never invented). State: `PENDING_CANCEL` (confirm_id → {user_id, order_id, total, service_name, quantity}). Hooks: at the top of the `intent_type == "cancel"` branch — explicit cancels run ONLY when no pending of ANY kind is open (an open card owns the word "cancel"); bare "cancel" with a PENDING_CANCEL open drops that card; bare "yes" confirms a cancel (acceptance block, after the refill branch). `on_callback` tail entry after PENDING_REFILL (pop PENDING_CANCEL by arg; cancel → "cancelled"; confirm → `_run_cancel_order_confirm`). 7 new TEXT keys.
- **What changed — server/api-order-cancel.php (new).** Mirrors `api-order-rescue.php`'s provider-cancel logic with renamed helpers (`can_` prefix). POST /orders/{id}/cancel {telegram_id} — ownership + cancellable statuses pending/processing/in progress/queued; provider cancel via `godofpanel_call('cancel')` or direct fallback; re-check provider status; ONE transaction: mark cancelled, credit FULL stored total to auth.balance, insert `bot_order_cancels` (order_id UNIQUE) — duplicate → same refund, duplicate:true; completed → 409, nothing refunded. Dispatch footer + live curl test in the file.
- **What changed — test_bot_offline.py.** New `TestCancelOrderApiClient` + `TestCancelOrderInChat`: cancel last / by id shows the card with the full total; Confirm refunds the exact server figure; a completed order is refused with its real status; an unknown id → not found; bare "cancel" with nothing pending answers "Nothing to cancel."; 404 → "isn't switched on yet". `FakePaxyo` gained `cancel_order` + `order_records` + `order_status_none` params.
- **BUILT + VERIFIED offline 15 Nov 12:xx EAT (part of the 1328/1328 run). NOT pushed, NOT deployed.**
- **Server-side install steps (parent runs these on paxyo.et, DirectAdmin Terminal — same shape as the round-1 install):** 1. Upload `server/api-order-cancel.php` to `public_html/api/`. 2. `php -l api-order-cancel.php` — must pass. 3. Splice into `public_html/api/bot.php` BEFORE the 404 fallback: the dispatch lines from the bottom of the file, copying the `bot_rate_limit(...)` call from the orders handler onto them. 4. Append the file's code WITHOUT its first `<?php` line to the end of bot.php. 5. `php -l api/bot.php` — must pass before anything goes live. 6. Live curl test from the bottom of the file, then DELETE the test rows. Only after the endpoint answers 200 should the bot deploy go out — until then the bot answers "isn't switched on yet" and nothing is cancelled.
- **What to verify LIVE after the server install + deploy:** cancel a pending order → Confirm → provider cancels, full total back in the balance; cancel a completed order → honest refusal with its status; cancel someone else's order id → not found (no leaking); tap Confirm twice → one refund only.
- **Open questions / caveats:** (a) FULL-TOTAL REFUND vs the Terms' "remaining amount" wording — same tension as rescue A7: the endpoint refunds the FULL stored total on a user-initiated cancel. William must confirm that is the rule (vs. refunding only the undelivered part). (b) The provider-cancel success shape is assumed from the rescue file's pattern — the live curl test at install confirms it. (c) A cancel that succeeds at the provider but fails to credit locally is a 500 with nothing marked cancelled — the admin must reconcile by hand (same as rescue).

## 15 Nov 12:xx EAT — Round 3, feature 13: partial order handling (built, verified, NOT pushed/deployed yet)
- **What it does.** When a status check shows the provider delivered LESS than ordered (status "partial"), the bot tells the user honestly what arrived vs what was ordered and refunds the undelivered part to their Paxyo balance automatically, naming the exact refund. Refund = remains × total/quantity (server-computed, never in the bot). The order status stays "partial" — it is never marked completed or cancelled.
- **What changed — bot.py.** `PaxyoClient.partial_refund()` (POST /orders/{id}/partial-refund {telegram_id} → {ok, order_id, delivered, remains, refunded_etb, duplicate}; 404 → None; 409/502 → {ok:false, error}). `_maybe_partial_refund(app, user_id, order_id, quantity)` (returns the user-facing line or None; the server's exact refunded_etb, never computed locally; quantity falls back to delivered+remains when unknown; 404/409/502/exception → None, never raises). Delivery-watch branch after the completed branch: `elif status == "partial":` attempts the refund at most once per hour per order (`partial_attempted_at` on the watch entry); on a line, notify + pop the watch entry; otherwise keep watching. `last_order_status_text` hook: a "partial" status attempts the refund and returns the refund line instead of the bare status line; None falls through to the plain status line. 1 new TEXT key (partial_refunded). NOTE: "partial" was NOT handled before — it fell through and kept watching forever.
- **What changed — server/api-order-partial-refund.php (new).** POST /orders/{id}/partial-refund {telegram_id} — read provider status with remains via `godofpanel_call('status')` or raw fallback; only when status is "partial"; refund = remains × total/quantity rounded to 2dp, capped at the ordered quantity; `bot_order_partials` (order_id UNIQUE) — one refund per order, ever; order status stays "partial". Dispatch footer + live curl test in the file.
- **What changed — test_bot_offline.py.** New `TestPartialRefundApiClient` + `TestPartialRefund`: partial status → exact refund line with arrived vs ordered figures; duplicate attempt within the hour does not double-refund; non-partial status → no refund line; 404 → plain status line, nothing breaks. `FakePaxyo` gained `partial_refund`.
- **BUILT + VERIFIED offline 15 Nov 12:xx EAT (part of the 1328/1328 run). NOT pushed, NOT deployed.**
- **Server-side install steps (parent runs these on paxyo.et, DirectAdmin Terminal — same shape as feature 12):** upload → `php -l` → splice dispatch lines before the 404 fallback → append code without the first `<?php` → `php -l api/bot.php` → footer curl test → delete test rows. Only after the endpoint answers 200 should the bot deploy go out — until then a partial status shows the plain status line and no refund is attempted.
- **What to verify LIVE after the server install + deploy:** an order the provider delivered partially → the user gets the arrived-vs-ordered line + the exact refund in their balance; a second status check of the same order → no second refund; a completed order never triggers a partial refund.
- **Open questions / caveats:** (a) the provider status reply must carry `remains` (candidates remains/remain/remaining/undelivered) — if GodOfPanel phrases it differently, the live test at install shows it and the file's reader needs the real key. (b) Delivered is read from the reply, else quantity − remains. (c) The refund needs the order's stored quantity — an order row with no quantity cannot be partially refunded (409).

## 15 Nov 12:xx EAT — Round 3, feature 14: abandoned deposit nudge (built, verified, NOT pushed/deployed yet)
- **What it does.** A user who starts a deposit (method + amount given, Confirm card shown) but never finishes gets ONE gentle reminder in chat after 3 hours of quiet, with a one-tap Resume button that re-shows the same Confirm card. Never more than once per abandoned deposit; never to users who completed a deposit since starting it.
- **What changed — bot.py.** `DEPOSIT_ABANDONED` (telegram_id → {confirm_id, amount, method, phone, started_at, nudged_at}) recorded at PENDING_DEPOSIT creation in `handle_deposit_text`; `ABANDONED_DEPOSIT_HOURS = 3`. `run_abandoned_deposit_cycle(app, now=None)` mirroring `run_abandoned_nudge_cycle`, wired into `run_scheduler_cycle`: skip when the card is gone (confirmed/cancelled → prune); skip when already nudged; wait 3h of quiet; check `list_deposits` — if any completed deposit has created_at ≥ started_at (parsed with `_parse_order_created_at`), drop the record and never nudge; an unparseable completed-deposit date is treated as completed-since (err toward not nagging a payer); otherwise send the nudge with a Resume button (`abandoned_deposit_keyboard`, callback `resumedep:<confirm_id>`) and set nudged_at. `on_callback` `resumedep:` (look up PENDING_DEPOSIT by id; gone → abandoned_deposit_gone; else re-show the Confirm card with the SAME confirm_id — including the validated-promo line when VALIDATED_PROMO has a code — so the existing confirm/cancel tail handles it; pop DEPOSIT_ABANDONED). 3 new TEXT keys.
- **What changed — test_bot_offline.py.** New `TestAbandonedDeposit`: no nudge before 3h; nudge at 3h with the Resume button; Resume re-shows the same Confirm card; a completed deposit since → no nudge ever; an already-nudged deposit is never nudged twice; a confirmed/cancelled card prunes the record. (Second-precision created_at can truncate to just before started_at — the test uses +1 minute.)
- **BUILT + VERIFIED offline 15 Nov 12:xx EAT (part of the 1328/1328 run). NOT pushed, NOT deployed. Server-side changes needed: NONE** (uses the existing deposits list).
- **What to verify LIVE after deploy:** start a deposit, leave it 3h → one reminder with Resume; tap Resume → the same Confirm card; complete a deposit in between → no reminder; a second 3h wait → no second reminder.
- **Open questions / caveats:** (a) 3 hours is my choice, not William-approved — confirm or change ABANDONED_DEPOSIT_HOURS. (b) The record is in-memory: a restart between starting and the 3h mark loses the nudge (same restart caveat as the other in-memory cycles). (c) The nudge only covers deposits started in chat with a Confirm card — website deposits never enter this flow.

## 15 Nov 12:xx EAT — Round 3, feature 15: disputes inbox (built, verified, NOT pushed/deployed yet)
- **What it does.** "I have a problem with order #1841" files a dispute (order id, user, their words) into the admin side; the user gets a short "filed, we'll look at it" reply. The admin bot lists the open ones with `/disputes` and closes one with "close dispute #<id>" (Confirm first). Closing a dispute never refunds and never touches a balance, an order or the provider — a refund, if owed, is a separate step (the admin bot's refund flow, after Confirm).
- **What changed — bot.py.** NONE — the bot-side filing already worked: `is_report_problem_request` matches "problem with order" / "there is a problem", and the flow files via POST /support-reports (`server/api-support-reports.php`). A test proves it (see below).
- **What changed — admin_bot.py.** `disputes_cmd` (/disputes, registered, in help/CAPABILITIES) + `format_disputes` (one line each: id, order, user, words truncated to 120 chars; "Close one with: close dispute #<id>"). `parse_dispute_close_command` ("close dispute #3" — plain language only; "/disputes close 3" does NOT match, the regex needs "close dispute"). on_text branch 5b: looks the dispute up first (unknown id → "There is no open dispute #N."), stores PENDING kind "dispute_close" with `_dispute_preview`, executed in `_execute_pending` → "✅ Dispute #N closed." Client methods: `disputes()` (GET /disputes), `close_dispute(dispute_id)` (POST /disputes/{id}/close) — both 404-tolerant.
- **What changed — server/api-disputes.php (new).** GET /disputes → {disputes:[{id, order_id, telegram_id, username, message, status, created_at}], count} — open only, newest first, max 50, reading the SAME `bot_support_reports` table the reports file writes (created here too, same CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS, so the endpoints work even if the reports file was never installed). POST /disputes/{id}/close → {ok, id, status:"closed"} — conditional UPDATE on status='open', so an already-closed or missing id is a 404, never a silent ok. Dispatch footer + live curl test in the file.
- **What changed — tests.** bot: new `TestDisputesFiling` — "I have a problem with order #1841" files via report_problem with the right order id and the user gets the filed reply. admin: new `TestDisputes` — /disputes lists open ones; close is Confirm-gated; unknown id refused; 404 → "not connected yet". `FakeAdminPaxyo` gained disputes methods.
- **BUILT + VERIFIED offline 15 Nov 12:xx EAT (part of the 1328/1328 + 207/207 runs). NOT pushed, NOT deployed.**
- **Server-side install steps (parent runs these on paxyo.et, DirectAdmin Terminal — same shape as feature 12):** upload → `php -l` → splice dispatch lines before the 404 fallback → append code without the first `<?php` → `php -l api/bot.php` → footer curl test → delete test rows. No money moves: listing and closing never touch balances, orders or the provider.
- **What to verify LIVE after the server install + deploy:** file a problem in chat → /disputes shows it with the right order id + words; "close dispute #N" → Confirm → gone from the list; closing it again → honest "not found or already closed".
- **Open questions / caveats:** (a) the disputes list reads only `bot_support_reports` — problems filed any other way (e.g. Support Chat) do not appear here. (b) "handled" vs "closed": the reports table has a 'handled' status the close endpoint never sets — if the admin panel uses 'handled' for in-progress disputes, this list shows only 'open' ones, which is the intended inbox.

## 15 Nov 12:xx EAT — Round 3, feature 17: fraud watch (built, verified, NOT pushed/deployed yet)
- **What it does.** Suspicious patterns are flagged to the admin — never blocking a user automatically, only flagging. Server-side detection + one admin alert per user per day max. Three patterns: many orders in a short window (rapid-fire), repeated failed payments, and a burst of new accounts.
- **What changed — bot.py.** `PaxyoClient.fraud_watch()` (GET /fraud-watch → {flags:[...]}; 404 → None). `run_fraud_watch_cycle(app, now=None)` every `FRAUD_WATCH_CHECK_SECONDS = 900` (15 min), wired into `run_scheduler_cycle`: calls fraud_watch() and relays flags to the admin via `notify_admins`; the server claims one alert per user per day (server-side dedupe in `bot_fraud_alerts`); 404 → silent with a log warning. `_FRAUD_WATCH_LAST_CHECK` throttle state.
- **What changed — server/api-fraud-watch.php (new).** GET /fraud-watch → {flags:[{telegram_id, username, type, detail, count}]} — only flags that fired TODAY and have not been alerted yet today; calling again the same day returns an empty list (the alert is claimed at read time). Types: "rapid_orders" (≥ FRD_RAPID_ORDERS orders in the last 10 minutes by one user), "failed_deposits" (≥ FRD_FAILED_DEPOSITS failed deposits in the last 24h by one user), "new_account_burst" (≥ FRD_NEW_ACCOUNTS new accounts in the last hour — a burst, not one user: telegram_id null, claimed under telegram_id 0). Claiming: INSERT IGNORE into `bot_fraud_alerts` with UNIQUE (telegram_id, flag_type, alert_date) — only the flags whose insert landed are returned. Usernames best-effort from auth. Dispatch footer + live curl test in the file.
- **What changed — test_bot_offline.py.** New `TestFraudWatch`: flags relayed to the admin; empty list → nothing sent; 404 → silent, no crash. `FakePaxyo` gained `fraud_watch`.
- **BUILT + VERIFIED offline 15 Nov 12:xx EAT (part of the 1328/1328 run). NOT pushed, NOT deployed.**
- **Server-side install steps (parent runs these on paxyo.et, DirectAdmin Terminal — same shape as feature 12):** upload → `php -l` → splice dispatch lines before the 404 fallback → append code without the first `<?php` → `php -l api/bot.php` → footer curl test. The only write is the alert-claim rows in `bot_fraud_alerts` (the dedupe mechanism) — no balances, orders or users change.
- **What to verify LIVE after the server install + deploy:** on a quiet day the honest answer is an empty list; a real burst (5 orders in 10 min by one user) → one admin alert naming the user + count; the same pattern again the same day → no second alert; the next day → alerts again.
- **Open questions / caveats:** (a) THRESHOLDS ARE A PROPOSAL, NOT WILLIAM-APPROVED: 5 orders / 10 min, 3 failed deposits / 24h, 10 new accounts / 1 hour — William must confirm or change them (three constants at the top of the file, one line each). (b) "New accounts" is a count of auth rows created in the last hour — a burst proxy, not a "one source" proof; the server cannot see a shared source (IP/device) from the tables it has; real source tracking needs the signup path to record it first. (c) A failed deposit is status = 'failed' only — if the live table also uses 'rejected'/'cancelled', add them to the query. (d) The alert claim is date-based in server time (CURDATE()) — a flag at 23:55 and again at 00:05 alerts twice, once per calendar day, which is the approved rule.

Until the create endpoint stores cbebirr separately, the CBE Birr row will under-count and CBE will over-count. ## 15 Nov 12:xx EAT — Round 3, feature 19: uptime watch (built, verified, NOT pushed/deployed yet)
- **What it does.** The bot watches its own provider + paxyo.et API reachability on its scheduler cycle; if either fails twice in a row, one admin alert (throttled 1/hour); a recovery message when it comes back. Admin-only, never to users.
- **What changed — bot.py.** `run_uptime_watch_cycle(app, now=None)` using `admin_stats()` for the API and `provider_balance_usd` presence for the provider, 2 consecutive failures → one alert, 1/hour throttle per target (`UPTIME_STATE` {"api"/"provider": {fails, down, last_alert}}), recovery message when it comes back; throttled to one check per `UPTIME_CHECK_SECONDS = 300` (5 min); `UPTIME_ALERT_THROTTLE_SECONDS = 3600`; `_UPTIME_LAST_CHECK`. 404/None stats → watch stays off with a log warning, never an alert on a missing number. Wired into `run_scheduler_cycle`. **REAL BUG FOUND BY TESTS AND FIXED:** the throttle suppressed the first-ever alert because `last_alert` defaults to 0.0 and the synthetic test `now` values (1000–1600) were within 3600 of 0 — fixed in bot.py so `last_alert == 0.0` ("never alerted") skips the throttle; only a real previous alert throttles.
- **What changed — test_bot_offline.py.** New `TestUptimeWatch`: one failure → no alert; two in a row → one alert; a third failure within the hour → no second alert; recovery → recovery message; 404/None → no alert, no crash. `_SmartLoopBase` setUp/tearDown now re-inits UPTIME_STATE to its default shape (clear() would delete the "api"/"provider" keys the cycle reads) and resets `_UPTIME_LAST_CHECK` to −1e9.
- **BUILT + VERIFIED offline 15 Nov 12:xx EAT (part of the 1328/1328 run). NOT pushed, NOT deployed. Server-side changes needed: NONE** (reuses GET /admin/stats).
- **What to verify LIVE after deploy:** the watch is silent while the API + provider answer; if the provider goes unreachable twice in a row → one admin alert; when it answers again → one recovery message; a second outage within the hour → no second alert.
- **Open questions / caveats:** (a) "Provider unreachable" is inferred from a missing `provider_balance_usd` field in /admin/stats (the stats endpoint omits it on provider failure, never fakes 0) — so a stats outage and a provider outage both read as "API down" first; the provider check only runs when stats answer. (b) The 2-in-a-row rule needs two scheduler cycles (~10 min on the 5-min throttle) before the first alert — a 1-minute blip never alerts. (c) State is in-memory: a restart resets the failure count (one honest repeat alert if still down).

## 15 Nov 12:xx EAT — Round 3, feature 20: satisfaction score in admin (built, verified, NOT pushed/deployed yet)
- **What it does.** The 👍/👎 feedback from round 2's feedback check is aggregated into a score (% positive) shown in the admin bot with `/satisfaction` and as a line in the morning report. One row per order — a user who taps twice on the same order cannot count twice (the ratings endpoint enforces once per order, 409 after the first).
- **What changed — bot.py.** The feedback callback (`on_callback` `feedback:` branch) ALSO files a rating via `paxyo.rate_order(order_id, user.id, "good"/"bad")` best-effort (409 already-rated is safe; the existing feedback flow is unchanged).
- **What changed — admin_bot.py.** `satisfaction_cmd` (/satisfaction, registered, in help/CAPABILITIES) + `format_satisfaction_line` ("Satisfaction: 85% positive (17 of 20)"; total 0 → "Satisfaction: no feedback yet."). `format_morning_report(stats, satisfaction=None)` adds the satisfaction line when a record is given; `send_morning_report_once` and `morning_cmd` fetch `satisfaction()` best-effort (404 → line left out, money figures still go out). Client method `satisfaction()` (GET /satisfaction, 404-tolerant).
- **What changed — server/api-satisfaction.php (new).** GET /satisfaction → {total, positive, negative, percent_positive} — aggregates `bot_order_ratings` (rating 'good'/'bad'); percent_positive is the share of 'good', rounded to a whole number; with no ratings yet it is null (the admin bot says "no feedback yet" instead of a 0% that would read as all-bad). If the ratings table does not exist yet, the counts read 0 — the endpoint still answers honestly. Dispatch footer + live curl test in the file.
- **What changed — tests.** bot: new `TestFeedbackRating` — a 👍/👎 tap files the rating (good/bad) via rate_order; the feedback flow itself is unchanged. admin: new `TestSatisfaction` — /satisfaction shows the % line; zero ratings → "no feedback yet"; the morning report carries the line when the endpoint answers and omits it on 404. `FakeAdminPaxyo` gained satisfaction.
- **BUILT + VERIFIED offline 15 Nov 12:xx EAT (part of the 1328/1328 + 207/207 runs). NOT pushed, NOT deployed.**
- **Server-side install steps (parent runs these on paxyo.et, DirectAdmin Terminal — same shape as feature 12):** upload → `php -l` → splice dispatch lines before the 404 fallback → append code without the first `<?php` → `php -l api/bot.php` → footer curl test → delete test rows. Read-only: no money moves, no rows change.
- **What to verify LIVE after the server install + deploy:** /satisfaction shows the real % from the ratings table (cross-check: total must equal the row count of bot_order_ratings); a 👍 tap on a feedback ask increments the positive count; /morning carries the same line; before the install, /satisfaction answers "not connected yet" and /morning still sends the money figures.
- **Open questions / caveats:** (a) the score counts ratings only — a user who answers the feedback ask but never taps a rating button is not counted (the ask and the rating are the same tap, so this should be rare). (b) All-time only — no 7-day window (unlike the deposits report). (c) The morning-report line appears only when the endpoint answers; on a 404 the report goes out without it (by design — money figures must never be held hostage by a missing score).

Hook in `handle_order_text` right after the usual-request check, before the classifier. 1 new TEXT key (reorder_no_last). **"Same as last time but 500" is deliberately NOT in the new detector — the usual handler already takes it (explicit quantity wins there).**
- **What changed — test_bot_offline.py.** New `TestReorderNewQuantity`: "reorder my last order with 2000 views" → Confirm card with the old service + link + 2000; "same order but 500" → same; "order again 1000 tiktok views" → normal order, not a reorder; no last order → honest "no last order" reply. `FakePaxyo.recent` defaults to [] (RECENT_ORDER is only a class constant — reorder/cancel tests must pass recent=[...] explicitly).

## 15 Nov 12:xx EAT — Round 3, feature 23: remember deposit method (built, verified, NOT pushed/deployed yet)
- **What it does.** The bot remembers each user's last successful deposit method and offers it first ("Telebirr again?") in the deposit flow; a "use a different method" option is always shown. Picking "again" skips the method ask and goes straight to the phone step; picking "different" asks the method plainly and never offers the remembered one again for that deposit.
- **What changed — bot.py.** Trait saved on deposit completion in `deposit_status_text`'s completed path (method from `deposit.get("method")`, one of telebirr/cbe/cbebirr/mpesa; mirror via `_traits_entry(user_id)["last_deposit_method"] = method` + `schedule_traits_save(user_id, {"last_deposit_method": method})` — best-effort, throttled, never raises). In `handle_deposit_text`'s method-None branch: when amount is known and the user is NOT in `DEPOSIT_METHOD_DECLINED`, `_remembered_deposit_method(user_id)` (mirror first, else `load_traits` once, filling the mirror) offers it with `deposit_method_again_keyboard` ("✅ {method} again" / "Use a different method"); otherwise asks the method plainly. `on_callback` `depagain:<method>` (validate the slug, require an amount in DEPOSIT_DRAFT, set method, ask for phone) and `depdiff` (add user to DEPOSIT_METHOD_DECLINED, ask method plainly). DEPOSIT_METHOD_DECLINED is cleared when the deposit's Confirm card is shown. State: `DEPOSIT_METHOD_DECLINED` set. 3 new TEXT keys (deposit_method_again, deposit_method_again_button, deposit_method_other_button).
- **What changed — server/api-user-traits.php (EDITED).** `trt_validate_traits()` now accepts `last_deposit_method`: null clears it; a string must be one of 'telebirr', 'cbe', 'cbebirr', 'mpesa' — a junk value fails the whole save, like every other known field. Header trait set updated (5 → 6 fields) with the rule: the bot writes it only from a completed deposit's own method, never from a guess; picking "use a different method" does not clear it (the next completed deposit overwrites it). Footer curl test gained steps 6b/6c (save + invalid value). **This file was never installed live before — its first install carries this edit.**
- **What changed — test_bot_offline.py.** New `TestRememberDepositMethod`: a completed deposit saves the method as a trait; the next deposit offers "Telebirr again?" with the different-method option; tapping "again" skips to the phone step; tapping "different" asks the method plainly and never re-offers for that deposit; a user with no remembered method gets the plain ask. (`schedule_traits_save` creates a background task that reads the global client when it runs, so tests must flush while bot.paxyo is still patched.)
- **BUILT + VERIFIED offline 15 Nov 12:xx EAT (part of the 1328/1328 run). NOT pushed, NOT deployed.**
- **Server-side install steps (parent runs these on paxyo.et, DirectAdmin Terminal — same shape as feature 12, for the EDITED api-user-traits.php):** upload → `php -l` → splice dispatch lines before the 404 fallback → append code without the first `<?php` → `php -l api/bot.php` → footer curl test (incl. the new 6b/6c steps) → delete test rows. Until the traits endpoint is installed, the bot still remembers the method in-memory for the running process (the mirror) — the server copy is what survives a restart.
- **What to verify LIVE after the server install + deploy:** complete a Telebirr deposit → start a new deposit with an amount → "Telebirr again?" appears; tap it → phone step; tap "different" → plain method ask; restart the bot → the remembered method still offered (server copy).
- **Open questions / caveats:** (a) the remembered method is the last SUCCESSFUL deposit's method — a failed or abandoned deposit never overwrites it. (b) The trait is stored alongside the personality traits in `bot_user_traits`; clearing all traits ("forget what you know about me") also clears the remembered method.

## 15 Nov 12:xx EAT — Round 3, feature 25: promo code manager in admin bot (built, verified, NOT pushed/deployed yet)
- **What it does.** `/promo create CODE 10%` (percent or fixed ETB, optional expiry/max uses) / `/promo list` / `/promo off CODE` — server endpoint stores codes; the existing chat promo redeem path uses them. Create and off are Confirm-gated; list is a plain read.
- **What changed — admin_bot.py.** `create_promo_code` extended: `create_promo_code(code, percent=None, fixed_etb=None, expires_at=None, max_uses=None)` — percent optional now, body includes fixed_etb when given. `parse_promo_create_command` now parses a fixed amount ("100 ETB" / "100 birr" — not "100 uses") into `fixed_etb`; `validate_promo_create` enforces percent XOR fixed_etb (both → "Pick one bonus"; neither → "Tell me the bonus too — a percent… or a fixed amount…"); fixed must be ≥ 1 ETB. `_promo_preview` and the `_execute_pending` promo branch show the fixed bonus ("100.00 ETB bonus on a deposit"). New parsers `parse_promo_list_command` ("/promo list" / "list promo codes" / "show promo codes") and `parse_promo_off_command` ("/promo off CODE" / "turn off promo code CODE" / "disable promo code CODE"). `promo_cmd` handler: bare /promo explains, `list` shows every code, `off CODE` is Confirm-gated via new PENDING kind "promo_off" (executed in `_execute_pending` → "✅ Promo code X is now off."). on_text branches 5a (plain-language list/off) after the promo-create branch. `format_promo_codes` lists every code with bonus, active/off, expiry, uses. Client methods: `promo_codes()` (GET /promo-codes), `promo_off(code)` (POST /promo-codes/{code}/off). CAPABILITIES + help_cmd updated.
- **What changed — server/api-promo-admin.php (new).** GET /promo-codes → {codes:[{code, percent, fixed_etb, active, expires_at, max_uses, uses, created_at}]} — every code, active first then off, newest first; `uses` is the redemption count from `bot_promo_redemptions`. POST /promo-codes/{CODE}/off → {ok, code, active:false} — conditional UPDATE on active = 1, so an already-off or missing code is a 404, never a silent ok; the code stays in the table (history kept), it just stops being redeemable. `pma_ensure_table` creates `bot_promo_codes` (same shape as the codes file, incl. fixed_etb) and ALTERs the column in when the table exists without it. Dispatch footer + live curl test in the file. **This file does NOT create codes — creation is the existing POST /promo-codes in api-promo-codes.php.**

Header money rule + A7 + TABLES comment + footer curl test updated (steps 10–14: fixed create/validate/both/neither/redeem-with-cap; cleanup now deletes TEST10 + FIXED100). **This file was never installed live before — its first install carries this edit.**
- **What changed — test_admin_bot_offline.py.** New `TestPromoManager`: create percent / create fixed / both → refused / neither → refused / list shows codes / off is Confirm-gated and executes / unknown code → honest refusal. Existing promo expectations updated for the new contract (PENDING promo dict + promo_calls dicts now include "fixed_etb": None; missing-bonus refusal wording changed from "Tell me the percent too" to "Tell me the bonus too"). `FakeAdminPaxyo` gained promo_codes/promo_off + records.

The off test must use a TEST code — never switch off a live code for a test. The redeem steps in the codes file's footer credit a REAL bonus on a REAL completed deposit — small test deposit only, with William's approval.

That is the conservative default (keeps "never creates money from nothing" true for fixed codes). If he wants fixed bonuses paid in full regardless of deposit size, remove the `min()` in `prc_maybe_redeem()` step 4 — one line. Until he says so, the cap stays. (b) The max-bonus cap question from round 1 (A7) is still open for percent codes (a 100% code doubles a deposit). ## 15 Nov 12:xx EAT — Round 3, feature 27: weekly growth report (built, verified, NOT pushed/deployed yet)
- **What it does.** Admin bot `/weekly` + automatic Monday 8am EAT: users gained, orders, revenue, net profit vs the previous week, from the server. Weeks are EAT Monday–Sunday; the % change is shown where last week's figure exists.
- **What changed — admin_bot.py.** `format_weekly_report` (this week vs last week, EAT Monday–Sunday, % change via `changes` from the server or `_pct_change` fallback; last-week 0 → no % shown). `should_send_weekly(now, sent_this_week)` (Monday only, hour ≥ 8); `send_weekly_report_once(app, now=None)` mirroring `send_morning_report_once` (server marker GET/POST /admin/weekly-sent keyed on the Monday's date, in-memory `WEEKLY_SENT_MONDAYS` fallback; nothing marked when the endpoint is not connected); `_seconds_until_next_monday_8am_eat`; `weekly_report_loop`; `_post_init` now starts both loops. `weekly_cmd` (/weekly, registered, in help/CAPABILITIES) on demand. Client methods: `weekly_stats()` (GET /admin/weekly), `weekly_sent(monday_str)` / `mark_weekly_sent(monday_str)` (GET/POST /admin/weekly-sent).
- **What changed — server/api-admin-weekly.php (new).** GET /admin/weekly → {this_week:{users, orders, completed, revenue_etb, net_profit_etb, deposits_count, deposits_etb, from, to}, last_week:{...same...}, changes:{users_pct, orders_pct, revenue_etb_pct, net_profit_etb_pct}} — weeks are EAT Monday–Sunday; users = auth rows created in the week; orders = orders placed in the week (any status); completed = status 'completed'; revenue = SUM(total) of completed orders (same definition as admin-stats); net profit = revenue × 220/420; deposits = completed deposits (money in, NOT revenue). `changes.*_pct` is the percent change from last week, rounded to a whole number, or null when last week's figure is 0 (no division by zero, no invented "+infinity"). GET /admin/weekly-sent?date=YYYY-MM-DD → {date, sent}; POST /admin/weekly-sent {date} → {ok, date, sent} (idempotent). Table `bot_weekly_sent` (date DATE PK — the Monday, sent_at). Dispatch footer + live curl test in the file.
- **What changed — test_admin_bot_offline.py.** New `TestWeeklyReport`: /weekly shows this vs last week with % changes; Monday ≥ 8am sends once, other days/times do not; a restart after the send does not double-send (server marker); 404 → "not connected yet" and nothing marked. `FakeAdminPaxyo` gained weekly/weekly_sent methods; `AdminBotBase` setUp/tearDown clears WEEKLY_SENT_MONDAYS. (2026-11-16 is a Monday — used for the weekly gate tests.)
- **BUILT + VERIFIED offline 15 Nov 12:xx EAT (part of the 207/207 run). NOT pushed, NOT deployed.**
- **Server-side install steps (parent runs these on paxyo.et, DirectAdmin Terminal — same shape as feature 12):** upload → `php -l` → splice dispatch lines before the 404 fallback → append code without the first `<?php` → `php -l api/bot.php` → footer curl test → delete test rows. The weekly-sent POST in the footer test must use a PAST Monday (or delete the test row afterwards) — never this week's Monday before the real send.
- **What to verify LIVE after the server install + deploy:** /weekly shows this week vs last week with the right figures (cross-check this_week.orders against the orders placed since Monday 00:00 EAT); Monday 8am → one automatic report; a second /weekly the same Monday → the same figures, no double-send marker problem; a restart Monday after 8am → no second report.
- **Open questions / caveats:** (a) weekly % change when last week is 0 shows no % (not "infinite") — confirm that reads right to William, vs. showing "+100%" or similar. (b) Same 220/420 hardcode caveat as the morning report. (c) Same free-Render wake caveat as the morning report: on free Render the loop only runs while awake, so the Monday 8am send goes out on the next wake after 8am; exact 8am needs the always-on server (Oracle move). (d) The report counts bot + website orders together (same orders table) — there is no per-channel split.
