## 15 Nov 18:17–19:00 EAT — Second provider wiring: SMM Social Media live in @paxorder_bot (chat bot ONLY)

- **Scope (William, 18:44 EAT): CHAT BOT ONLY.** SMM ordering goes on @paxorder_bot only. The mini app / website ordering flow is NOT changed at all — the website stays exactly as it is. Users order SMM services in chat exactly like GodOfPanel services (same Confirm card flow, same chat UX). The new server PHP endpoint is only invisible backend plumbing so the chat's orders can reach the SMM API — it is not a mini-app feature. Everything user-facing lands in the chat bot. (Verified: all user-facing changes in this build are in bot.py; the server PHP is only chat-bot API plumbing; nothing in the mini-app/website ordering flow was touched.)
- **Pricing (William, 18:17 EAT): SMM ×400 ETB, GodOfPanel stays ×420 ETB.** SMM rates are in INR: server converts ÷94.263 to USD, then ×400. Never show provider names or buy prices to users — only Paxyo selling prices. Internal provider slugs in bot.py are short: "gop" / "smm" (the full names never appear in bot.py source — enforced by TestSecrecy.test_godofpanel_nowhere_in_module). The server files use the canonical full names (not user-facing, not covered by that test). The bundled catalogue file's provider field values ("godofpanel"/"smmsocialmedia") are data, mapped to slugs at load time.
- **Picking (William, 15 Nov 15:32 EAT + 18:46 EAT): fastest by claimed average time across BOTH providers, one merged list.** The bot loads `combined-services-2026-11-15.with-times.json` (10,476 entries, combined_id "gop:<id>"/"smm:<id>"; 2,790 bare IDs collide, always order by provider+id). `PaxyoClient.services()` merges the live API list (GOP rows win on clash) with the bundled file's SMM entries; missing bundle → GOP-only, like before.
- **Cross-provider fallback chain (William, 18:46 EAT): BOTH providers work together so customers get fewer failed and stuck orders.** The bot picks the fastest service across GodOfPanel and SMM; if that order stalls OR fails, it cancels/refunds and moves to the next-fastest service even if that one is on the OTHER provider — and keeps going until the order completes. The existing stall-rescue logic was extended across providers rather than keeping each provider's chain separate. Standing design kept: top-3 by claimed average time, fastest first; MAX_STALL_RESCUES = 2 (3 attempts total, bot.py:7595) — **DECISION POINT for William: raise the cap now that the pool spans two providers, or keep 3 attempts as the safety limit against infinite refund/re-place loops on a bad link.**
- **Bot changes (bot.py):** helpers `service_provider()` / `service_combined_id()` / `normalise_combined_entry()` / `load_combined_catalogue()` / `merge_service_catalogues()`; constants GOP_SELL_MULTIPLIER_ETB = 420, SMM_SELL_MULTIPLIER_ETB = 400, SMM_INR_PER_USD = 94.263. New client methods: `create_order_smm()` (POST /orders/smm, no total sent — server computes it), `order_status(order_id, telegram_id, provider=None)` (provider "smm" reads GET /orders/{id}/smm-status, None on 404), `create_delayed_order(..., provider=None)`, `fail_refund(order_id, telegram_id)` (POST /orders/{id}/fail-refund, None on 404, raises otherwise). `create_order_for_service()` is the provider-aware placement wrapper, used at all placement sites: place_pending_order, place_batch, _attempt_stall_rescue, run_one_delayed, run_one_schedule. `find_service_by_id()` / `resolve_named_service()` accept combined ids; blacklist is GOP-only (an SMM service sharing a blacklisted bare id is NOT excluded). `_next_rescue_service()` takes combined ids in failed_services (bare ints read as GOP). Delivery watch passes the watched service's provider to order_status. Price watch snapshot keys are combined ids (old bare-int keys migrate to GOP); SMM margin is price×200/400 (GOP price×220/420) and SMM changes are labelled "#smm:<id>". Delayed orders carry provider (default GOP) + derived combined_id; delayed list / run_one_delayed resolve by combined id first. Browse keyboards emit `browse:<combined_id>` (bare-id taps still work). NUDGE_SERVICE / USUAL_SERVICE pins and Friday patterns store combined ids; Friday accept/same-link callbacks split from the right (`rsplit(":", 1)`) because the combined id itself contains a colon. Usual learning learns the combined id; `_usual_fallback()` accepts a combined-id string or an old bare int.
- **Failed-order rescue (NEW, this build — William's 18:46 EAT "or fails").** The watch used to drop failed orders silently. Now status "failed" triggers `_attempt_failed_rescue()`: refund via POST /orders/{id}/fail-refund, then re-place on the next-fastest service across providers via `create_order_for_service`. Rescue ONLY "failed" — NEVER "cancelled"/"canceled" (could be the user's own cancel via the round-3 cancel feature; re-placing it would undo their cancel) or "refunded" (already refunded). Outcomes: rescued → `order_failed_rescued` message; refunded with no replacement (cap reached / no next service / balance too low) → `order_failed_refunded`; endpoint not live (404) → `order_failed_no_refund` once, entry stays watched, hourly retry (FAIL_REFUND_RETRY_SECONDS = 3600, like the partial-refund throttle) — if the endpoint is installed later, the next retry refunds + rescues; provider disagrees (409: live word is not failed — the order may still complete) → keep watching, retry hourly, after 2 conflicts send `order_failed_unclear` once and keep watching (a late completion still gets its delivery message). The cap limits RE-PLACEMENT attempts, never the refund — a chain that already rescued twice still gets its money back when the third order fails. New strings contain no banned words ("provider" is banned in user-facing text — the first draft used it and TestSecrecy caught it).
- **Server files (in `~/workspace/paxyo-bot/server/` unless noted):**
  - `api-order-smm.php` (941 lines, NEW): SMM endpoint. Constants SMM_INR_PER_USD = 94.263, SMM_SELL_MULTIPLIER_ETB = 400; `smm_provider_key()` (constant SMM_API_KEY, then getenv, else null); `smm_provider_raw()` (query-string params, POST empty body, 30s timeout); `smm_services()` / `smm_find_service()`; `smm_place()`; `smm_order_status()`; `smm_cancel()`; `smm_refill()`; `smm_order_provider()` / `smm_is_smm_order()` (safe when table missing); table `bot_smm_orders` (order_id BIGINT PK, provider VARCHAR(32) DEFAULT 'smmsocialmedia', provider_order_id VARCHAR(64), provider_service_id INT, created_at). `bot_smm_place_order()` (POST /orders/smm): validates telegram_id/service_id/link/quantity/idempotency_key (400s); quantity must be a multiple of 10; account must exist in auth (404); live SMM list validates service + min/max (400); total computed server-side `rate_inr / 94.263 * 400 * quantity / 1000`, rounded 2dp, client total never read; 409 Insufficient balance; ONE transaction debits + inserts order (status 'pending', idempotency_key UNIQUE — duplicate 1062 rolls back and returns the earlier order with `duplicate:true`); then SMM add; on success writes provider id to first existing of api_order_id/provider_order_id/provider_id + inserts bot_smm_orders row; on provider failure refunds + marks failed in one transaction, 502 with the provider's reason. Reply `bot_ok(['ok'=>true,'order'=>['id'=>N,'status'=>'pending','total'=>X,'provider'=>'smmsocialmedia'],'duplicate'=>false])`. A total rounding to 0.00 ETB is a free order (no debit, no refund needed). `bot_smm_order_status()` (GET /orders/{id}/smm-status): ownership check (wrong owner → same 404 as missing); non-SMM order → 404; reads live SMM status; when the provider word differs from stored orders.status, UPDATES the stored status to the provider's word (lowercased). File footer contains dispatch lines, bot.php patch snippet, and live curl tests.
  - `api-order-cancel.php` (+27, 631 lines): SMM branch — `can_provider_cancel_smm()` wrapper + `$is_smm` branch in `bot_can_cancel_order()`.
  - `api-order-rescue.php` (+29, 642 lines): SMM branch — `res_provider_cancel_smm()` wrapper + `$is_smm` branch; covers cancel + status re-check live inside `smm_cancel()`.
  - `api-order-refill.php` (+30, 488 lines): SMM branch — `ref_provider_refill_smm()` wrapper + `$is_smm` branch; SMM unsupported-action → 501 `refill_supported:false`.
  - `api-order-partial-refund.php` (+40, 541 lines): SMM branch — `par_provider_status_smm()` wrapper + `$is_smm` branch; refund math unchanged.
  - `api-order-fail-refund.php` (523 lines, NEW): POST /orders/{id}/fail-refund {telegram_id}. Self-contained (own `frf_*` helpers; uses only bot_ok() + global $db from bot.php; does NOT depend on the stall-rescue file's helpers). Flow: existing fail-refund row → return it with duplicate:true; load order + ownership check (404); stored completed/cancelled/refunded → 409; re-reads the LIVE provider status itself (GOP via godofpanel_call() with a direct-call fallback, SMM via smm_order_status() guarded by function_exists); refunds the FULL stored total ONLY when the live word is failed/canceled/cancelled — NO provider cancel call (the provider already ended it); any other live word → 409 (partial → hint to use the partial-refund path); provider unreachable → 502 (bot retries). One transaction: re-check bot_order_fail_refunds under lock, mark order 'refunded' (UPDATE allows status IN ('pending','processing','in progress','inprogress','queued','failed') — 'failed' MUST be in the list), credit auth.balance, insert the row. Table `bot_order_fail_refunds` (order_id BIGINT UNIQUE, telegram_id, service_id, refunded_etb DECIMAL(12,2), provider_status, provider_order_id, created_at). Footer has dispatch line + live curl tests.
  - `api-delayed-orders.php` (edited at `~/workspace/paxyo-bot/api-delayed-orders.php`, 449 lines): provider VARCHAR(32) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'godofpanel' in CREATE TABLE + best-effort ALTER in `del_ensure_tables()` (try/catch); `bot_del_create()` accepts optional "provider" (canonical godofpanel/smmsocialmedia AND short slugs "gop"/"smm", normalised server-side — the chat bot must never carry the full provider names); `del_has_provider_column()` (SHOW COLUMNS) decides whether INSERT includes the column; if column missing and provider is SMM → refuses 500 honestly; `del_row()` returns provider (old rows read as godofpanel); list/due return it.
  - `server/api-user-traits.php` (edited, small additive change — NOT in the server subagent's list, flag for review at install): `usual_service_id` now also accepts a combined-id string "gop:<id>"/"smm:<id>" (stored verbatim); bare ints still accepted (GOP).
  - Branch guards (all four edited files): each computes `$is_smm = function_exists('smm_is_smm_order') && smm_is_smm_order($db, $order_id)` after loading the order row, then routes the provider call to the `smm_*` helper; otherwise the GOP path runs byte-identical. If api-order-smm.php is not installed yet, `smm_is_smm_order()` doesn't exist, `$is_smm` is false, and an SMM order falls through to the GOP path and fails honestly with the provider's error — never silently.

SMM: Step 0 — read the balance and re-check the live SMM rate first. (1) Add — SMM #7013 Instagram Views, qty 100, idempotency_key "test-smm-1" (use a real Instagram post link William owns, not the placeholder). Snapshot rate 0.074 INR/1000 → total `0.074/94.263*400*100/1000 = 0.03 ETB`. Expect `{"success":true,"ok":true,"order":{"id":N,"status":"pending","total":0.03,"provider":"smmsocialmedia"},"duplicate":false}` (total follows the live rate — the server's total is the authority). (2) Status — `GET /orders/N/smm-status?telegram_id=779060335`. (3) Duplicate idempotency key — same POST, same key → same id:N, `"duplicate":true`, balance unchanged apart from the one order total. (4) Wrong-owner 404 — `GET /orders/N/smm-status?telegram_id=1` → `{"success":false,"error":"Order not found."}`. (5) Insufficient-balance 409 — pick a quantity whose total exceeds the balance (snapshot: SMM #2016 YouTube views, rate 235.857 INR/1000, min 1000, qty 2000 → `2001.70 ETB`, above the ~1,075 ETB operating balance; idempotency_key "test-smm-2"). Expect HTTP 409 `{"success":false,"error":"Insufficient balance."}` and balance unchanged. (6) Bad service id 400 — service_id 999999999, qty 100 → HTTP 400 `{"success":false,"error":"Service not found."}`. (7) Non-SMM order 404 — `GET /orders/<known-GOP-order-id>/smm-status?telegram_id=779060335` → 404. Cleanup: cancel the test order via POST /orders/N/cancel (SMM branch refunds in full), then `DELETE FROM bot_smm_orders WHERE order_id=N;` and confirm 0 rows. The cancelled order row itself stays, status `cancelled`. Fail-refund: needs a test order that FAILED at the provider (place one on a service known to fail, confirm status reads "failed" first) — (1) confirm failed, (2) POST fail-refund → expect `{"success":true,"ok":true,"order_id":N,"refunded_etb":<total>,"provider_status":"failed","duplicate":false}`, (3) balance back up by exactly the total, (4) repeat → same refunded_etb with `"duplicate":true`, balance unchanged, (5) wrong owner → 404, (6) still-pending order → 409 "Provider status is ..., not failed", (7) completed order → 409. Then `DELETE FROM bot_order_fail_refunds WHERE order_id=N AND telegram_id=779060335;` and confirm 0 rows (the order row stays, status `refunded`).

(8) SMM balance is only $0.78 USD — real SMM orders need a top-up first. (9) Full-total vs remaining-amount refund wording (rescue A7) is still open for William — the fail-refund endpoint refunds the full stored total, same as the stall rescue; settle before go-live if the policy changes, and change it in both files.
- **What was NOT done (belongs to the main agent):** no live order placed, no deploy, no push to GitHub, no touch of live paxyo.et. Server install + bot deploy + live test order (<5 ETB, approved) are the next steps after this build.

## Rescue price cap — never charge more than confirmed (owner rule, 15 Nov 19:16 EAT)

- **Rule (William, 15 Nov 19:16 EAT): the bot must NEVER charge a customer more than the amount they confirmed.** A rescue replacement must cost the same or less than the original confirmed total. Before this fix the rescue picked the next service by fastest only, refunded the full original total, then placed the replacement if the balance covered it — so a customer who confirmed 5 ETB could be charged e.g. 19 ETB without being asked. William: "They click confirm just only for 5 br and you charge them like more then 3 times which is not acceptable."
- **What changed — bot.py, pick.** `_next_rescue_service(services, service, failed_services, max_total=None, quantity=0)` — new optional price cap. When `max_total` is set it returns the fastest AFFORDABLE service (estimated total from `rate_etb_per_1000` × quantity, via `estimated_total_etb`) across BOTH providers, skipping pricier candidates; same price counts as affordable ("at most"). The candidate limit rose from 3 to `RESCUE_CANDIDATE_LIMIT = 10`, because pricier candidates are skipped and affordable services further down the list must still be found. With no cap (`max_total=None`) the pick is the old fastest-first one — existing direct callers are unchanged. New helper `_rescue_price_cap(watched, service, quantity)`: the cap is the watch entry's stored confirmed total (`watched["total"]`), falling back to the original service's catalogue estimate when the entry has none; after a rescue the replacement's own total becomes the cap for the next rescue, so the cap only ever stays the same or shrinks down a chain.
- **What changed — bot.py, stall path (`_attempt_stall_rescue`).** The pick is now price-capped at the confirmed total. When it finds no affordable service, the function checks the uncapped pick: none at all → existing behaviour (order left alone, stuck warning, no cancel); services exist but all pricier → `cancel_refund` still runs (full refund), then new template `order_rescue_pricier` tells the user the refund figure and the plain reason ("The next services cost more than the {refunded} ETB you confirmed, so I did not move your order"), the watch entry is dropped, and the function returns True so the caller sends no stuck warning. The balance check after the refund stays as a guard (it normally passes now — the replacement costs at most the refunded amount). Attempts still count actual placements only: skipping a pricier candidate is not an attempt.
- **What changed — bot.py, failed path (`_attempt_failed_rescue`).** Same price-capped pick after the fail-refund. When no affordable service remains: if the uncapped pick exists (services exist but all pricier) → new helper `_refunded_pricier()` sends new template `order_failed_refunded_pricier` (refund figure + plain reason) and drops the entry; if no service exists at all → existing `_refunded_no_replacement()` (`order_failed_refunded`). The cap check (`rescue_count >= MAX_STALL_RESCUES` → refund only) still runs first. No Confirm card is added by this change — the rule is a hard cap, not an ask-first flow.
- **What changed — bot.py, templates.** Two new templates, en + first-pass am (unreviewed), same style as the other rescue messages: `order_rescue_pricier` (stall side) and `order_failed_refunded_pricier` (failed side). The existing `order_rescued` / `order_failed_rescued` messages are unchanged and still show both totals — the new total is now never more than the refunded one. The `order_rescued` template comment was updated to say so. No provider names in bot.py user-facing strings — secrecy test still passes (grep 0).
- **What changed — test_bot_offline.py.** FakePaxyo gained per-order refund figures (`cancel_refund_results={"1841": {...10.0...}, "1842": {...5.0...}}`) so rescue chains refund each order's own stored total. 8 net new tests (suite 1,363 → 1,371, ALL PASS, 83.1s, 15 Nov ~19:24 EAT): TestStallRescue — `test_next_rescue_service_skips_pricier_for_cheaper` (direct pick: pricier skipped, same price affordable, none when all pricier, no-cap pick unchanged), `test_pricier_next_service_skipped_for_cheaper_further_down` (stall end-to-end: 20.00 skipped, 5.00 taken, rescue_count 1), `test_all_remaining_pricier_refunds_and_tells_user` (stall end-to-end: full refund, no placement, clear message, no stuck warning, entry dropped), `test_same_price_replacement_still_works`, `test_rescue_chain_skips_do_not_consume_attempts` (chain skips the pricier service twice, still gets both re-placements, cap stops the third); TestFailedRescue — `test_failed_order_all_remaining_pricier_refunds_with_reason`, `test_failed_gop_order_skips_pricier_smm_for_cheaper_gop` (cross-provider: pricier SMM skipped, cheaper GOP taken). Two existing tests were updated to the new behaviour: `test_all_three_fail_final_message_then_stop` → replaced by `test_chain_stops_at_pricier_refund` (chain stops at the pricier refund, not a third placement) plus `test_all_three_affordable_fail_final_message_then_stop` (same chain with all-affordable services still runs to the cap-exhausted final message); `test_failed_smm_order_rescued_on_gop` fixture gained an affordable GOP service (7003 @ 4.00) so the pick skips the pricier fastest GOP (7002 @ 10.00) and takes it.
- **What was NOT done (belongs to the main agent):** no live order placed, no deploy, no push to GitHub, no touch of live paxyo.et. The price cap is bot-side only — no server file changes. It rides the next bot push/deploy; the live SMM test order (<5 ETB, approved) still waits on the server install (SMM_API_KEY first).

## Split-test for big orders + the +10% Paxyo band (owner, 15 Nov 19:32–19:55 EAT)

- **Settled method (William, 15 Nov 19:32 EAT, corrected 19:37 EAT, approved to build 19:41 EAT).** For orders whose confirmed total is **over 400 ETB** (`SPLIT_TEST_THRESHOLD_ETB = 400`): place a **10% test** on the FASTEST service first (across both providers, using the existing fastest pick), then the remaining **90% on the SAME service** once the test delivers. If the test gets stuck or fails: cancel/refund the test, then try the NEXT service **cheaper first** with the same 10% test, repeating down the cheaper services. If a cheaper service's test succeeds, place the remaining 90% there — the TOTAL quantity delivered is NEVER more than ordered, even if the cheaper price would allow more views for the same money — and REFUND the money difference (confirmed total minus actual cost) to the user's Paxyo balance. If NO cheaper service works, a pricier service may be used, but only **up to 10% above the confirmed total** (`SPLIT_COVER_MAX_FRACTION = 0.10`), the extra coming from Paxyo's pocket, never from the customer. Default method, customisable per customer later (no per-customer settings built here).
- **Small orders (William, 15 Nov 19:52 EAT, re-confirmed 19:53 EAT): 400 ETB or less — NO test order.** The WHOLE order goes at once on the fastest service first; if stuck/failed, rescue cheaper-first (the cheaper tier — services at or below the confirmed total — before the +10% band), then up to +10% pricier with the extra from Paxyo's pocket. The +10% tolerance is part of BOTH paths. The quantity cap (never more than ordered) and the refund-of-difference rule apply to small orders the same way (a small-order rescue refunds the full original total first, then places the cheaper replacement — the saving is the money never re-spent).
- **Rescue cap SETTLED (William, 15 Nov 19:55 EAT): 5 tries for a normal order (was 3 attempts total).** `MAX_STALL_RESCUES = 4` — the original placement plus up to 4 re-placements. One "try" is one service attempt. **Counting rule for split-test:** a test order plus its remainder on the SAME service counts as ONE try (the remainder is the completion of that try, not a new one — `_place_split_remainder` watches the remainder with the test leg's own `rescue_count`); a stalled test that moves the bot to the next service uses up one try (`_attempt_split_test_rescue` watches the new test with `rescue_count + 1`). The cap limits RE-PLACEMENT attempts, never the refund itself.
- **Recording (settled 19:43 EAT): test orders are REAL provider orders the customer paid for, so they MUST be recorded in the orders table and visible in the customer's mini-app history.** The bot marks them with split-test idempotency keys (`split-test-<hex>` / `split-rest-<hex>`, 43 chars, under the server's 64-char truncation) and split fields (`split_group_id`, `split_part` "test"/"remainder", `confirmed_total_etb`, `paxyo_cover_etb`) — the server stores the markers on the order row (see the server file below). A refund of the price difference shows as a visible balance credit (the cancel-refund of a stalled test leg IS a real balance credit; the saving from a cheaper success is money never charged — see Money flow). **ALSO: William gets an admin-bot message for EVERY stuck test order** (order id, service, quantity, cost, what the bot did next) via the existing `notify_admins` path, so he can cancel manually if the automatic cancel fails — including when the cancel/refund endpoint is not live yet or the provider will not confirm the failure (those paths send the admin message with "Not refunded yet"). If `ADMIN_TELEGRAM_IDS` is unset, the alert is skipped gracefully like existing alerts.
- **Money flow — the +10% mechanism (design Q1: a server change IS needed).** The bot cannot mint money: the server debits the user's balance atomically at placement, and the balance only holds the confirmed total. New file **`server/api-order-split-cover.php`** (340 lines, rides a later install, NOT installed): `bot_split_cover_ensure_tables()` (CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS `bot_split_covers`: id, split_group_id, order_id UNIQUE, telegram_id, cover_etb, confirmed_total_etb, created_at), `bot_split_cover_check()` (cumulative cover for the group + this leg ≤ 10% of confirmed total), `bot_split_cover_record()` (idempotent per order_id, called ONLY after the provider confirms the placement — a provider failure refunds the debit, so the cover was never spent), `bot_split_cover_status()` (GET /split-cover/status → `{"ok": true, "split_cover_live": true}` — the bot's capability probe), `bot_split_cover_add_columns()` (ALTER TABLE orders ADD split_group_id / split_part / paxyo_cover_etb with SHOW COLUMNS guards). Splice instructions in the file's footer for bot.php POST /orders and api-order-smm.php `bot_smm_place_order` (five edits each: read + validate the four optional fields; debit total − cover; store markers on the order row; refund total − cover on provider failure, NOT total; record the cover only after provider confirmation). **The bot probes `split_cover_live()` (cached per process) BEFORE using the cover band; if not live, pricier-than-confirmed services are treated as unaffordable (strict-cap behaviour) — safe degradation.** The cover is honoured WITHOUT split markers too (a small-order rescue sends confirmed_total_etb + paxyo_cover_etb with no group id). The server validates cover ≤ 10% of confirmed total (and ≤ 10% cumulatively per split group) and refuses more with 400.
- **Money flow — the refund of the difference.** The confirmed total is NEVER debited upfront (the server debits only actual placed costs), so the saving from a cheaper service is money that was never charged — there is no separate refund transaction to record, and recording one as a credit would double-pay the user. It is made visible by (1) the bot's message naming the exact saved figure (template `order_split_saved`: "You saved {saved} ETB — the service cost less than the {total} ETB you confirmed, so the difference stays in your balance."), (2) both order rows (test + remainder) in the mini-app history with their totals, (3) the balance reflecting only actual spend. The cancel_refund of a stalled test leg IS a real visible balance credit. **Flag for William:** if he expects a separate refund transaction line for the saving, say so — the current design shows it as money never spent, not as a credit.
- **Money flow — worked example (10,000 views, confirmed 500.00 ETB).** Test 1,000 on fastest (50.00) → delivers → remainder 9,000 same service (450.00): total debited 500.00, total delivered exactly 10,000. Cheaper service wins (40.00/1000): test 40.00 + remainder 360.00 = 400.00 for the same 10,000 views — 100.00 never charged, named in the delivery message. No cheaper works: pricier service 550.00 total (exactly +10%): test leg cover 5.00 + remainder leg cover 45.00 = 50.00 recorded; server debits 500.00 (550.00 − 50.00); customer still pays only 500.00. A service beyond +10% is never sent by the bot and refused 400 by the server.
- **Design Q2 — rounding vs minimums.** Quantities MUST be multiples of 10 (server enforces; bot enforces at parse). `split_test_quantities(quantity, service)`: test_qty = max(ceil(10% of Q) rounded UP to a multiple of 10, service min rounded UP to a multiple of 10); rest_qty = Q − test_qty; total is exactly Q. Returns None (no split, place whole on the non-split path) when Q is not a multiple of 10, when rest ≤ 0, or when rest < min. The retry pick (`_next_split_service`) skips candidates that cannot be split.
- **Design Q3 — stall detection.** Reuses the existing watch machinery — `is_order_stuck` (elapsed ≥ 3× average AND ≥ 15 min overdue; no average time = never stuck). A test merely slow but within its claimed average time is NOT stuck.
- **Pick order — fastest vs cheapest (decided in this build).** The split-test retry is cheapest-first (`_next_split_service` sorts candidates by total ascending; the first within the band is the cheapest one there). The SMALL-order rescue keeps the established fastest-first pick within the affordable tier (cheaper tier = every service at or below the confirmed total, tried before the +10% band; within the band the pick is cheapest-first, so Paxyo pays the least extra). Reason: the standing fulfilment rule is fastest-first, the cross-provider test pins fastest-first rescue picking (`test_next_rescue_service_cross_provider`: smm:874 @40.00 beats gop:1861 @1.51 with no cap), and fastest-vs-cheapest is a per-customer setting William approved separately (order customizations #4, 19:57 EAT — NOT built here), not a change to the default. New helper `_rescue_with_cover(services, service, failed_services, quantity, confirmed_total, allow_cover)` implements the two tiers and returns (service, cover); `_next_rescue_service` gained an optional `cheapest_first=False` parameter used only for the band pick.
- **What changed — bot.py, placement.** `place_pending_order`: after the balance check, when the confirmed total is over 400 ETB and `split_test_quantities` returns a split, `_place_split_test` places the 10% test (fresh `split-test-<hex>` key, split markers, `watch_order` with split context leg "test", `rescue_count` 0) and returns `split_test=True` in the result; both summary call sites (`send_order_result` and the Confirm-tap handler) append the `order_split_test_placed` note. A service the order cannot be split on falls through to the normal whole placement. Small orders (≤ 400) take the normal path unchanged. `create_order_for_service(..., split=None)` passes the split/cover kwargs through to both provider endpoints; `PaxyoClient.create_order` / `create_order_smm` send `split_group_id` / `split_part` / `confirmed_total_etb` / `paxyo_cover_etb` only when set; new `PaxyoClient.split_cover_live()` (GET /split-cover/status, 404-tolerant → False, cached per process).
- **What changed — bot.py, scheduler (`run_delivery_watch_cycle`).** Completed branch: a test leg routes to `_place_split_remainder` (no delivery message yet — the order is not finished); a remainder leg gets the normal delivery message with the FULL ordered quantity (test + rest) and the `order_split_saved` line when the saving is > 0 (no saving line when cover was used). Stall branch: a test leg routes to `_attempt_split_test_rescue` (the cap is handled inside it — a test at the cap is refunded, not left "still in progress" — so the exhausted check is skipped for test legs); other legs use the existing stall rescue with the new cover-aware pick. Failed branch: a test leg routes to `_attempt_split_test_rescue(..., failed=True)`; a remainder leg uses the plain failed rescue with split context carried forward.
- **What changed — bot.py, split rescue (`_attempt_split_test_rescue`, `_place_split_remainder`).** `_place_split_remainder`: balance check first (needs rest cost − cover), cover-live gate when cover > 0, remainder placed with a `split-rest-<hex>` key on the same service, watched as leg "remainder" with `spent` = the delivered test's cost; every failure path tells the user honestly and drops the test entry (the test portion stays delivered and paid, the rest is simply not ordered). `_attempt_split_test_rescue`: pick BEFORE the refund (so a cover pick can check cover-live first); balance pre-check (balance + about-to-land refund must cover the new test after cover); refund via cancel_refund when stalled / fail_refund when failed (hourly throttle, conflict counting, `order_failed_unclear` after 2 conflicts, mirroring `_attempt_failed_rescue`); admin message via `notify_admins` on every stuck test (including cancel-not-live / provider-disagrees paths); cap → refund + `order_split_cap_refunded`; no service within the band → refund + `order_split_no_service`; next test watched with `rescue_count + 1` and the failed service recorded by combined id. **Nothing returns False after the refund** — once the money is back, the entry is always resolved with a plain message, never left to the generic stuck warning. All "cannot proceed" outcomes (services fetch failed, refund endpoint not live, refund raised/throttled, balance check failed, cover not live) happen BEFORE the refund and return False.
- **What changed — bot.py, plain rescue (small orders + remainder legs).** `_attempt_stall_rescue` and `_attempt_failed_rescue` now pick via `_rescue_with_cover` (cheaper tier, then the +10% band when `split_cover_live()` is true); the balance check is against `new_total − cover`; the placement carries the cover via `_rescue_cover_kwargs` (`confirmed_total_etb` + `paxyo_cover_etb` on top of any split markers); the rescued message appends `order_rescue_cover_note` ("Paxyo covers the extra {cover} ETB — you still pay only the {confirmed} ETB you confirmed.") when cover > 0, so the higher new total is never read as a charge. **The band does NOT apply to a split remainder leg** (its cover fields would overwrite the split group's confirmed total on the order row — `_rescue_cover_kwargs` would clobber it — so a remainder rescue keeps the strict cap of the remainder leg's own total). The `order_rescue_pricier` / `order_failed_refunded_pricier` messages now mean "beyond the confirmed total PLUS the 10% band". `order_rescue_exhausted` now says "four times" (was "twice"), en + am.
- **What changed — bot.py, helpers.** `SPLIT_TEST_THRESHOLD_ETB = 400`, `SPLIT_TEST_FRACTION = 0.10`, `SPLIT_COVER_MAX_FRACTION = 0.10`; `_ceil_to_10`, `_service_min_qty`, `split_test_quantities`, `split_leg_cover` (leg cover = leg cost − proportional share of confirmed total), `_next_split_service` (cheapest-first, split-ability filter, cover band), `_split_replacement_kwargs` (split markers for a remainder-leg replacement), `_split_test_admin_text` (admin message; `refunded=None` → "Not refunded yet — please cancel it manually."), `_rescue_with_cover`, `_rescue_cover_kwargs`, `_failed_combined_ids` (factored out of `_next_rescue_service` — shared by the rescue and split picks). `watch_order` gained a `split=None` parameter, stored on the entry. 10 new templates (en + first-pass am): `order_split_test_placed`, `order_split_test_done`, `order_split_saved`, `order_split_test_moved`, `order_split_no_service`, `order_split_exhausted`, `order_split_cap_refunded`, `order_split_rest_failed`, `order_split_rest_topup`, `order_split_rest_no_cover`, plus `order_rescue_cover_note`.
- **Scope — what the split does NOT cover.** Split applies in `place_pending_order` (the Confirm path — text/voice/button acceptance all funnel through it) only. Delayed orders (`run_one_delayed`), bulk orders, and recurring orders place directly via `create_order_for_service` and keep the non-split price-cap rescue — known limitation / follow-up. The round-3 cancel feature stays PARKED (the SMM cancel branch must not be installed/used until William says so — fail-refund is unaffected, it makes no cancel call).
- **What changed — test_bot_offline.py.** FakePaxyo gained `split_cover_live` (seedable, default False — the strict-cap behaviour until the endpoint is "installed"), `order_split_kwargs` / `smm_order_split_kwargs` (the split/cover kwargs of every order created, one dict per order, only the kwargs actually passed), and `create_order` / `create_order_smm` accept the four split kwargs. 22 net new tests in `TestSplitTest` (suite 1,371 → **1,393, ALL PASS**, 82.3s, 15 Nov ~20:10 EAT): helper math (`test_split_test_quantities`, `test_split_leg_cover`, `test_rescue_with_cover_tiers`, `test_next_rescue_service_cheapest_first_flag`); placement (`test_big_order_splits_ten_ninety_on_fastest`, `test_small_order_places_whole_no_split` incl. exactly-400, `test_big_order_on_unsplittable_service_places_whole`); scheduler flows (`test_test_delivers_places_remainder_same_service`, `test_remainder_delivers_full_quantity_no_saving`, `test_stalled_test_moves_to_next_cheaper`, `test_cheaper_success_refunds_difference_quantity_exact` — 200.00 saved, quantity exactly 10,000, `test_no_cheaper_uses_pricier_within_ten_percent` — leg covers 5.00 + 45.00 = 50.00, `test_pricier_beyond_ten_percent_refunds_no_placement`); admin alerts (`test_stuck_test_alerts_admin_with_details`, `test_admin_alert_skipped_without_admin_id`, `test_stuck_test_alerts_admin_when_cancel_not_live`); small-order cover (`test_small_order_rescue_uses_cover_within_ten_percent` — cover 1.00 on an 11.00 replacement for a 10.00 order, cover note in the message, `test_small_order_rescue_cover_not_live_keeps_strict_cap`, `test_small_order_rescue_beyond_ten_percent_refunds`, `test_small_order_rescue_cheaper_tier_first`, `test_small_failed_order_rescue_uses_cover`); cap (`test_five_try_split_chain_stops_with_refund` — 4 rescue tests, 5 refunds, cap message for the fifth, `test_test_plus_remainder_counts_as_one_try` — remainder rescue is rescue_count 1 with the same group id). Existing tests updated for the 5-try cap: `test_rescue_chain_skips_do_not_consume_attempts` (fixture extended to 6 services, 4 re-placements + exhausted), `test_all_three_affordable_fail_final_message_then_stop` → `test_all_five_affordable_fail_final_message_then_stop` (fixture extended to 5 services, 4 rescues + exhausted), `test_failed_order_at_rescue_cap_refunds_only` (rescue_count 2 → 4); TestStallRescue class docstring updated. Two bugs found by the new tests and fixed in the build: `watch_order`'s docstring lost its closing `"""` (suite would not import), and `FakePaxyo.create_order`/`create_order_smm` did not accept the split kwargs `create_order_for_service` passes unconditionally (42 failures until fixed). Secrecy: `grep -c -i godofpanel|smmsocialmedia bot.py` = 0.
- **Install order (belongs to the main agent — NOT done here).** (1) Add `SMM_API_KEY` to `public_html/config_secret.php` FIRST (the SMM wiring from the previous build still waits on it). (2) `php -l` `server/api-order-split-cover.php` on the server (no php binary in the VM — it was NOT linted here; `php -l` on the server is MANDATORY). (3) Apply the SPLICE edits in the file's footer to bot.php POST /orders and to api-order-smm.php, then `php -l` both files — a parse error in bot.php breaks the whole Bot API. (4) Insert the dispatch line for GET /split-cover/status before the 404 fallback, append the file's code WITHOUT its opening `<?php`. (5) Push the bundled combined catalogue JSON (`combined-services-2026-11-15.with-times.json`) + redeploy the bot (the SMM services stay dormant until then). (6) Run the live curl tests in the server file's footer (probe live; cover above 10% refused 400; cover within 10% debits total − cover; one cover record per order; group cumulative cap). (7) Live SMM test order under 5 ETB (approved 18:17 EAT) + a live split-test order over 400 ETB only with William's approval (it moves real money). **Note:** the bot's `split_cover_live()` probe caches per process — after the server install, the bot must be redeployed (or restarted) before it will use the cover band.
- **What was NOT done (belongs to the main agent):** no live order placed, no deploy, no push to GitHub, no touch of live paxyo.et. NOT built here: the order customizations William approved 19:57 EAT (#4 fastest-first vs cheapest-first, #6 ask-first vs just do it, #8 max slowness allowed) and 20:07 EAT (#11–#20: default link, default quantity, price ceiling per 1,000, refill-only services, drip-feed, auto-repeat, top up the missing part instead of only refunding, place small orders without asking, quiet hours, delivery deadline) — separate build, needs its own brief. The rescue-cap question is now SETTLED (5 tries) and the small-order +10% rule is built — do not re-ask either.

## Order customizations — per-customer settings (15 Nov 2026)

Built by the customizations coordinator, 15 Nov 2026, on top of the split-test code (live commit 1a61485). **Suite 1,393 → 1,441, ALL PASS (72.5s).** Secrecy: `grep -c -i godofpanel|smmsocialmedia bot.py` = 0. No live order placed, no deploy, no push, no touch of live paxyo.et.

- **Approved and built (29 options).** The original 13: #4 fastest-first vs cheapest-first (default stays fastest-first), #6 ask-first vs just do it (default stays auto-rescue; ask-first asks before moving a stuck order and waits for Yes/No — `rescue_yes:<id>` / `rescue_no:<id>` buttons, proposal stored on the watch entry), #8 max slowness (never use a service slower than the limit), #11 default link, #12 default quantity per order type, #13 price ceiling per 1,000, #14 refill-only, #15 drip-feed (provider `add` with `runs` + `interval`; quantity is the per-run quantity; total = qty × runs, charged up front; a drip order is placed WHOLE, never split-tested, and the watch cycle never stall-rescues it), #16 auto-repeat (reuses the recurring-orders endpoints; GOP-only — the server's recurring row carries no provider column, so SMM repeats are skipped), #17 top up the missing part (orders the missing quantity on the same service, paid from the refunded part plus at most 10% of the confirmed total from Paxyo's pocket, only when the cover is live; otherwise refunds and says so), #18 place small orders without asking (explicit opt-in only, cap the customer names up to 100 ETB hard max, confirmed back in chat when set, never for drip orders or big orders), #19 quiet hours (proactive messages held in `QUIET_HELD` and flushed in the morning with a `quiet_morning` header; admins exempt), #20 delivery deadline (only picks services that claim to make it, or warns and offers "place it anyway"). Plus the later-approved options: #21 favourite services (preferred among the picks), #24 low-balance alert, #25 weekly spending limit (refuses an order that would take the week's spending over the limit, using `recent_orders`), #26 one-tap usual order (already covered by the existing usual flow — no new code), #27 pause my repeats (`list_recurring` → `pause_recurring` each; resume has NO server endpoint — honest "not set up yet" reply), #30 watch my post and top up, #31 saved links list, #32 best time to deliver (names the fastest service's average time), #33 wait for a lower price, #34 auto-cancel if too slow (cancel + refund past the limit, before the rescue attempt), #35 monthly report, #36 start fast only (average time ≤ 15 min), #37 language lock (overrides detected language + bridge), #38 double confirm for big orders (second Confirm tap when the total is over the set amount), #39 never use these services (personal ban list, excluded in the candidate filter), #40 try new services (prefers picks with no claimed time yet).
- **NOT approved — never built, never pretended (11 options).** #1 split test on/off, #2 test size, #3 the 400 ETB line, #5 how much extra allowed, #7 how many tries, #9 pin one service, #10 spending cap, #22 split across services, #23 delivery report, #28 price alert for my usual service, #29 all or nothing. The parser recognises them and answers with the honest line (`not_available` action, `_NOT_AVAILABLE_TEXT`): the test is always 10% of orders over 400 ETB, the bot tries up to 5 services then refunds, one order goes on one service, a short delivery is refunded for the missing part, and so on.
- **Not switched on yet (remembered + stored, honest reply).** #30 (no live-count API exists — provider status gives only undelivered "remains", so a real watch cannot be built honestly now), #33 (needs per-user waits against the global price snapshot; no user registry server-side), #35 (needs a monthly scheduled send + aggregation). Same pattern as drip-feed before its server file landed.
- **Owner decisions folded in (20:30 EAT).** (1) Small-order rescue priority is FAST FIRST — the next fastest service that fits the confirmed price, not the cheapest (already how the code did it; pinned by `test_rescue_pick_fastest_first_vs_cheapest_first`: A 2 min @9.50 beats B 3 min @8.00 by default, cheapest-first customers get B). (2) Saving message: when a cheaper service wins, the delivery message says "we got a better deal and the rest of the money returned to your balance" (template `order_split_saved`, en + am, in the user's language via `LAST_LANG`); the money itself is unchanged (never charged, stays in the balance — no separate refund line). Pinned by template tests en + am and a full Amharic delivery-watch integration.
- **Storage — prefs.** New pref keys under the `pref_` prefix, in the `USER_TRAITS` mirror + server `bot_user_traits` table (same merge shape as traits: null clears, unknown dropped). `validate_pref` / `get_pref` / `set_pref` / `clear_pref`, `_save_prefs_now` / `schedule_prefs_save` (5s throttle), `_restore_prefs` (once per process per user, called from `_restore_traits`). `PaxyoClient.save_prefs` / `load_prefs` (POST/GET `/user-prefs`, 404-tolerant) and `dripfeed_live()` (GET `/orders/dripfeed-status`, cached per process). `filter_pref_candidates(services, user_id, deadline=None, skip_ceiling=False)` returns (kept, blocked_by) with blocked_by in ("max_slowness", "price_ceiling", "refill_only", "deadline", "banned", "start_fast"); unknown average time is excluded by a time filter; `skip_ceiling=True` is for rescues (the confirmed-total cap enforces the ceiling there). Deadline is a per-order draft field, not a standing pref; drip-feed is per-order (runs/interval on the draft), not a standing pref. `parse_deadline`, `parse_drip_span`, `drip_plan` (tries 10, 5, then 2 runs; per-run qty a multiple of 10 and ≥ service min; interval ≥ 30 min). Constants: `MAX_NO_CONFIRM_ETB = 100`, `DEFAULT_NO_CONFIRM_MAX_ETB = 50`, `QUIET_DEFAULT = ("22:00", "07:00")`, `START_FAST_MAX_MIN = 15`, `PREF_MAX_SLOWNESS_MAX_MIN = 10080`.
- **Chat handling.** `parse_pref_command` / `parse_pref_commands` (two-at-once: splits on " and " / ", ", max 3, only set/clear/set_quantity/set_repeat combine into one reply; anything else falls back to the first action alone) and `handle_settings_text` (called in `handle_order_text` right after the forget-traits check, before everything else; returns True/False). "what are my settings" lists one line per set pref (`pref_settings_text`) or the empty-state text naming the normal way; "reset my settings" clears all (checked BEFORE show). Only explicit settings phrasing matches — an order message never does. Ambiguous phrases ("just do it", "just refund") only count as a setting when the pref is actually on and no Confirm card is waiting. The classifier also has a "settings" intent (SYSTEM_PROMPT) with a dispatch fallback in `handle_order_text` for phrasings the parser misses. Settings replies are plain English; the chat bridge translates for Amharic/Tigrinya/Oromo users. `TYPO_MAP` extended with settings words (cheepest, fastist, veiw, folowers, alwasy, mesage, servise, repet, refil, confrm, chnage, befor, smal, delivre, usualy, quantitiy, nevr, pls, u).
- **Order-flow hooks.** `complete_order`: the automatic pool is filtered by `filter_pref_candidates` (named/pinned/last-order services are the user's explicit choice and are NOT filtered); cheapest-first rides the picker's "cheapest" hint word; favourites/try-new fetch 10 picks and prefer via `_prefer_pick` (ask-metric still uses the top 3); weekly-limit check after the total is computed (refuses, draft kept); low-balance alert line appended to the card notes; no-confirm path places at once via `send_order_result` (pending marked `"no_confirm": True`, summary says "Placed without asking, as you set for small orders") — never for drip orders; drip plan shown as a card note; deadline shown as a card note. Multi-turn flow: default-link fill (platform-checked), default-quantity fill (before ask-quantity), deadline/drip parse from raw text, "place it anyway" / "place it normally" clear them. `place_pending_order`: drip branch places whole with `runs`/`interval` (only sent when `dripfeed_live()` says the server honours them). `_maybe_start_auto_repeat` (dedup by platform+metric through the catalogue, GOP only) hooked into `send_order_result` and the Confirm callback. Rescue attempts (`_attempt_stall_rescue` / `_attempt_failed_rescue` / `_attempt_split_test_rescue`) filter candidates with `skip_ceiling=True`, pass `user_id` through so cheapest-first also reorders the affordable rescue tier, and propose (ask-first) before refunding (stall/split-test) or after refunding (failed). Watch cycle: drip orders skip the stall branch; auto-cancel (#34) runs right after the `is_order_stuck` check; partial branch tops up via `_maybe_topup_missing` when the pref is on. `notify_user` holds messages in quiet hours; `run_quiet_flush_cycle` (called last in `run_scheduler_cycle`) sends the morning batch.
- **Drip-feed provider support (researched 15 Nov).** Both providers support it via standard SMM panel API v2: `runs` (≥ 2) + `interval` (minutes) on the normal `add` call. GodOfPanel: 6,297 of 7,175 services have `dripfeed: true` (live snapshot `~/workspace/custom-connectors/custom.godofpanel/services-2026-11-12.json`); SMM Social Media: only 14 of 3,301 — in practice a GodOfPanel feature. Sending drip params to a service without the flag returns an error — the flag is checked first (bot refuses, server refuses with 400). One small live test order (under the 5 ETB cap, with William's approval) is still needed as final proof, since GodOfPanel's own API doc page sits behind login.
- **Training dataset (William, 21:10 EAT).** `golden-dataset/05-customizations-english.md` (623 items) + `06-customizations-amharic.md` (377 items) = **exactly 1,000** "User:" lines, every item with a "Bot should" line, 0 duplicates, 0 provider-name mentions, 43 sections each. Generated by `golden-dataset/generate_customizations.py` (seed 20261115, deterministic; slot pools + ~18% typo/voice-to-text rate). Covers all 40 options (most examples for the approved ones), asking about current settings, changing one, turning one off, setting two at once, and the 11 not-approved options with their honest replies. **Wiring follows yesterday's pattern exactly:** the whole dataset does NOT go in the prompt — 3 settings examples were added to `FEW_SHOT_EXAMPLES` (now 31 lines, 1,664 chars < 1,700, max line 85 chars < 100) plus a "settings" intent in SYSTEM_PROMPT (11,412 chars < 11,500; words×1.33 = 2,372.72 < 2,400 — trims taken from the deposit, how_to, source_question, paused_services and how_to_order lines; one reseller trim was reverted because a test asserts the exact phrase). The rest of the dataset lives in the offline tests. Parser spot-check (live flow = normalize + parse): 530/623 EN lines parse; the 93 that do not are expected non-parser items (30 drip-feed → `parse_drip_span`, 13 auto-repeat → classifier "recurring", 20 deadlines → `parse_deadline`, 15 usual → usual flow, 10 cheapest-orders → orders by design, and a few with no actionable detail).
- **Tests.** Two new classes, 48 tests (`TestOrderCustomizations` — prefs core, settings chat, order flow incl. drip branches, ask-first propose/Yes/No/default, rescue pick order, saving wording en + am, top-up placed/too-expensive/off, auto-cancel, quiet hold/flush, auto-repeat create/dedupe/SMM-skip, prefs persistence, default link/quantity fill; `TestCustomizationDataset` — file counts, parser samples for the new options in English, handler replies for pause/best-time/watch/wait/monthly-report, not-available recognition + honest replies, Amharic set + turn-off through the translate bridge with mocked translation, settings-intent dispatch, few-shot bounds, two-at-once, secrecy). **Bug found by the new tests and fixed in the build:** `parse_pref_command("turn off cheapest first")` returned set-cheapest — the #4 OFF branch sat after the SET branch, which matches the "cheapest first" substring first; the OFF check now runs first (also covers "stop cheapest first"). Two test-side assertion fixes in the same pass: `pref_no_confirm_cap` returns (False, 0) when off (the 50 default applies only once opted in), and the no-link ask text is "Please send the link of the video."
- **Bug fixed during the build (settings replies crashed).** `handle_settings_text` was called with a user id and passed it to `reply_message`, which needs a Telegram message object — every settings reply raised `AttributeError: 'int' object has no attribute 'reply_text'` and, since `on_text` has no try/except, the user got NO reply at all for any settings message. Fixed by passing `update.message` through (signature now `handle_settings_text(message, user_id, text, lang, voice)`).
- **Server files (NEW, under `server/`, ride a later install — NOT installed).** `server/api-user-prefs.php` — mirrors `api-user-traits.php` exactly, `prf_` prefix, same `bot_user_traits` table, functions `bot_prf_save` / `bot_prf_load`, merge semantics, validates all 25 pref keys (incl. the 13 new ones: favourites/banned id lists, low-balance alert, weekly limit, double-confirm, auto-cancel, start-fast/try-new/monthly-report booleans, language en|am|ti|om, saved_links, watch_post, wait_price); dispatch POST/GET `/user-prefs`; curl test in the footer (incl. a new-key save + a bad favourite id → 400). `server/api-order-dripfeed.php` — mirrors `api-order-split-cover.php`: probe GET `/orders/dripfeed-status`, splice into POST /orders (bot.php) and POST /orders/smm (`bot_smm_place_order`): optional `runs` (≥ 2) + `interval` (≥ 1 min; bot enforces ≥ 30); quantity is the PER-RUN quantity; total charged = per-run qty × runs (total, balance check, debit and insert all use qty × runs); the service's `dripfeed` flag is checked first and a drip request on a service without it is refused with 400; new nullable columns `drip_runs` / `drip_interval` / `drip_per_run_qty` (SHOW COLUMNS guards); runs/interval forwarded to the provider `add` call; provider failure refunds the full debit. When the fields are absent the handler behaves exactly as today. **Both files need `php -l` on the server before install (no php binary in the VM — NOT linted here; brace/paren balance checked by script only).** The +10% band and drip-feed both stay dormant until their server files are installed (bot uses strict cap / honest refusal meanwhile).
- **Known limitations.** Auto-repeat is GOP-only until the server recurring row carries a provider column. Drip-feed is effectively a GodOfPanel feature (14 of 3,301 SMM services). #30/#33/#35 are remembered but their doing part is not switched on yet. #27 resume has no server endpoint. QUIET_HELD is in-memory (a restart loses held messages — same as other in-memory state). The saving from a cheaper service is money never charged, not a separate refund transaction (see the split-test section's Money flow note).

## Admin training dataset — 1,000 English questions (15 Nov 2026, William 21:19 EAT)

- **What it is.** Same idea as the user-bot training datasets, but for the admin chat bot: exactly 1,000 realistic questions/messages William would send to @atlasdeskadmin_bot, ALL in English, with typos and voice-to-text style. `golden-dataset/07-admin-english.md` (18 sections: money 100, deposits 85, orders 85, users 80, services 65, broadcasts 80, promo 80, disputes 55, win-backs 45, loyalty 35, satisfaction 40, fraud 35, uptime 35, confused 30, reports 55, settings 40, resellers 20, general 35) + deterministic generator `golden-dataset/generate_admin_questions.py` (seed 20261115; `python3 generate_admin_questions.py` regenerates it byte-for-byte).
- **What was wired into admin_bot.py (existing pattern only — deterministic parsers FIRST, AI fallback, no new training mechanism).** (a) Net-profit questions: new `profit` kind in `parse_stats_question` + `answer_stats` (from the stats record's `net_profit_etb`; "not available yet" when the endpoint does not report it) + Net profit line in the summary. (b) One order's status in plain words: new `parse_order_status_command` ("what is the status of order #1841", "check order #1841", "where is order #1841" — refund/cancel wordings are refused so a refund is never read as a status check) + read-only `_order_status_text`, new on_text step 5c. (c) Plain-language report routes: new `parse_admin_report_command` mapping words to the existing /command handlers (profitable, satisfaction, weekly, deposits_by_method, disputes, morning, winbacks, help), new on_text step 5d — each runs the matching handler, so behaviour is exactly the command's. (d) Typo tolerance: `_ADMIN_TYPO_MAP` + `_normalize_admin_text` (same idea as the order bot's TYPO_MAP) applied to the detection copy in the stats, broadcast, refund, promo, dispute-close, order-status and report parsers; ticker keyword is typo-tolerant (chnage, tickr) + "announcement" means the ticker. (e) Broadcast triggers widened to "message"/"tell"; promo list accepts "show me the promo codes"/"list all promo codes"; dispute close accepts "resolve". (f) Stats-parser guard: pending / big / threshold deposits, per-user deposit counts and anything about the provider return None, so the AI says honestly it cannot look them up instead of answering with the wrong figure. (g) `ADMIN_AI_SYSTEM` gained the examples + the cannot-do list (no order/user lists, no single-user balance, no resellers/loyalty/fraud/confused lists, no provider balance, no cancel from chat, only the ticker changes in chat, no service prices quoted).
- **What the bot honestly cannot do in chat (taught, not invented):** list orders or users, look up one user's balance, list resellers, loyalty bonuses, fraud flags or confused chats, check the provider balance or uptime, change the price multiplier, quote service prices, cancel an order — those live in the admin panel; the bot says so and never invents a figure.
- **Tests.** `test_admin_bot_offline.py` grew by 31 (207 → 238, all green): `TestProfitQuestion`, `TestProfitAnswer`, `TestOrderStatusParsing`, `TestOrderStatusFlow`, `TestAdminReportRouter`, `TestAdminReportFlow`, `TestParserWidening`, `TestAdminDataset` (1,000 items / 18 sections / 0 duplicates / no provider names / regenerates deterministically / **every item routes to its section's behaviour**), `TestCommandNamesValid` (the hyphen regression test). Secrecy: grep 0 for `godofpanel|smmsocialmedia` in admin_bot.py and in the dataset.
- **Deploy note.** Nothing pushed or deployed from this build. Parent pushes + Render deploys atlas-desk-bot. The `/deposits_by_method` hyphen fix rides the same deploy (see the round-3 failure note above).
